**Result:** ✅ the prediction was REFUTED, and that was worth more than a confirmation. The waste went 30 → **14**, not 0 — and the number of repeatedly-burned delivered frequencies went UP (8 of 11 against 7 of 8). Cause, measured not guessed: under undervolt the card SAGS, so different ordered frequencies converge on one delivered frequency with no governor involved at all. The defect was a property of CONVERGENCE, not of the trap's fiction. Checked on a card with no trap in it: the ordinary rehearsal wastes **24 burns of 89 — 27 % of the band**. The epic's phase 2 had been written to fix the narrow case and would have left most of the cost in place.
**Lesson:** **a mutation exists to test the mechanism you NAMED, and when it fails to behave, the finding is that you named the wrong mechanism — not that the mutation was badly built.** The trap-first discipline is usually described as «prove the trap can go red»; its more valuable half is «prove you know WHY it is red», because the why is what the next phase aims at. The cheap discriminator, and it is the one that generalises: after the mutation fails to green, run the SAME measurement on a subject that has none of the trap's fiction — if the number survives there, the defect is broader than the epic that framed it, and the fix's scope must widen before a line is written. Corollary: a mutation whose result merely confirms the plan teaches nothing; budget the surprise, and treat «the mutation misbehaved» as the session's most informative event rather than an obstacle to route around.   → link: `plans/48` (находка 2) · `plans/47` (прицел фазы 2 исправлен) · `plans/49` · EXP-0150 (the neighbour: a mutation reddening ZERO measures absent coverage)
**Repro:** `npm run traps` → 30 wasted burns; set `governorBelowCeilingMhz: 0` in the trap card → 14, not 0; then `npm run bench -- --from 2857 --to 2790` on the untrapped card → 24 of 89.
**Trigger:** a plan predicts «mutation X turns the assertion green» → run it before trusting the phase's framing, and if it does not, re-measure on a subject without the trap's fiction before writing the next phase.
**Not for:** mutations aimed at a guard's own wiring (does this line still get called) — there a refuted prediction usually is just a broken mutation.

### EXP-0153 · 2026-08-25 · ❌→✅ · #requirements #acceptance-criteria #meters #headline-number #observation
**Context:** an epic opened with a headline number the owner had seen and approved: «4 wasted burns of 11 — 36 % of the card's time». Its acceptance criterion E47-AC1 carried that number as the «today» value, with a Scale line naming how it is measured. The next phase's whole job was to drive it to zero.
**Tried / did:** before building the counter, computed all four candidate meters over the run's own journal instead of trusting the summary table the number came from.
**Result:** ✅ the four meters gave **2, 2, 4 and 10**. The criterion's TITLE («a burn that gave no new depth») and its SCALE line both measured **2 of 11**; the headline «4» was a THIRD quantity — «the card did not serve the voltage we ordered» — which is a real fact and a different one. One rung had ordered 820 and been served 840: the card refused the order, and the burn was still 5 mV deeper than anything before it, i.e. it bought depth. Title, scale and target had been three different things, and nobody had run the meter.
**Lesson:** **a criterion's «today» number is a measurement and must be taken with its own meter — an approved headline is not a baseline.** The failure is quiet because every one of the candidate quantities is true; they just answer different questions, and the one that reaches the summary is whichever the author noticed first. Two mechanical consequences. First, when writing Scale/Meter/Target, compute the target's «before» value WITH the meter you just wrote, in the same sitting — if you cannot run it yet, the criterion is not finished. Second, when a criterion's title and its scale line can be read as different quantities, that ambiguity WILL be resolved by whoever implements it, silently and possibly differently from you. Third, and it is what made this cheap: the run's own journal had held the answer for an hour, and the whole check cost one script over data already on disk.   → link: `plans/48` (находка 1) · `plans/47` → E47-AC1 · `REQUIREMENTS_FRAMEWORK.md` → the fit criterion · EXP-0146 (the cheapest observation is the one already on disk)
**Repro:** take any acceptance criterion carrying a «today: N» number; write the meter its Scale line describes, run it over the artefact the number came from, and compare.
**Trigger:** about to build the fix for a criterion whose baseline you did not measure yourself → measure the baseline with the criterion's own meter FIRST, and correct the criterion if they disagree.
**Not for:** criteria whose baseline is an owner's perception verdict (the taste class) — there the number is the owner's eye and there is no meter to re-run.

### EXP-0152 · 2026-08-25 · ✅ · #recon-before-code #planning #guards #owner-directive #measurable-payoff
**Context:** the owner ordered a behaviour change (the descent must follow the frequency the card DELIVERS, not the one we ordered) after a live run showed 4 burns of 11 bought no new depth. The change looked small — «re-target the next rung» — and the obvious move was to open the descent loop and edit it. He stopped that: *«не за код берись, а за план — код для разведки, где не так»*.
**Tried / did:** spent the time reading instead of editing: the descent loop, the rung's return shape, the ladder builder, the harvest, and the progress guard. Wrote the findings down as a numbered list BEFORE any plan text.
**Result:** ✅ six findings, and one of them inverts the fix. The «no progress» guard keys repeats by VOLTAGE ALONE. After re-targeting, the same voltage on a different delivered frequency is legitimate new knowledge — so the guard would have closed the frequency early, and **the fix would have made runs SHORTER while looking correct**. Two more of the same family: the proven ground is a voltage with no frequency attached, and the step ladder is keyed to depth from the ORDERED frequency's stock, which differs on the delivered one. All three are invisible from the loop's own body.
**Lesson:** **when a change makes an existing quantity mean something new, the danger is not the code you edit — it is every guard that was keyed to the OLD meaning and will now fire on legitimate work.** Those guards live nowhere near the diff, so a coding-first session never meets them; it meets their damage later, as «the run got shorter», and blames the hardware. The cheap discriminator: before editing, list every place that KEYS off the quantity you are about to redefine — grep the field name, not the function — and ask of each «is its key still the whole identity?». Second half, and it is what makes this recordable rather than pious: the payoff was MEASURABLE and named before the work — one finding that would have silently inverted the fix's effect, found in reading time, at zero cost on the owner's card.   → link: `plans/47` (findings F1–F6) · `bugs/42` (the guard in question) · `bugs/54` (half a pair) · `AGENT_GUIDE.md` → Planning discipline
**Repro:** re-target a descent to the delivered frequency without touching the progress guard → the second rung on the new target trips «ступень заказывается ВТОРОЙ РАЗ» and closes the frequency.
**Trigger:** about to change what an existing field MEANS (its identity, its key, its anchor) → inventory every consumer that keys off it before writing the change, and treat each stale key as part of the change, not as a follow-up.
**Not for:** additive changes that introduce a new field and leave every existing meaning intact — there the consumers cannot be stale.

### EXP-0151 · 2026-08-25 · ❌→✅ · #truth-mirror #canon-drift #routing-documents #retired-rules #weak-session
**Context:** a routing document exists so a context-less session can operate the machinery without re-deriving the method — «want X → run Y, expect Z». It is the one file such a session reads BEFORE touching the owner's hardware.
**Tried / did:** while re-reading it for an unrelated reason, checked one of its numbers against the code instead of trusting it. It stated the shipping margin as «failure + 2 grid steps = +10 mV (owner's final word)». The owner had re-anchored that rule eight days earlier — to «last stable + ONE minimum grid step» — and the code not only computes it that way, it THROWS with an explanation if handed the doubled cushion.
**Result:** ✅ found and corrected. The reason it survived is the dangerous part: **where the card's voltage grid is 5 mV, both formulas give the SAME number.** The grid is non-uniform — 10 mV in 32 places of 126 — and exactly there the retired form collapses to zero and points at the rung that already passed. So the error was invisible in three quarters of the range and wrong in the quarter nobody checked. The canon's own summary file had been corrected two days earlier; its sibling router was never swept.
**Lesson:** **correcting a rule in the document where it was DISCOVERED does not correct it where it is READ.** A retired rule survives longest in routing/operating documents, because they are written to be terse and are consulted under time pressure — nobody re-derives a one-line rule they came there to look up. Two mechanical consequences: when an owner re-anchors a rule, the fix is a SWEEP of every document that states it (grep the old number AND the old anchor word, not the rule's name), and a routing document's numbers deserve the same truth↔mirror treatment as code — with the code as the truth and the doc as the mirror. Third, subtler: **a formula that agrees with its replacement on the common case is the hardest kind to catch**, so check such rules on the case where they DIVERGE, never on a typical one.   → link: `GPU_TUNING_RAILS.md` · `config.marginAboveLastStableMv` · `STATUS.md` fact 32 · `AGENT_GUIDE.md` → the truth↔mirror pairs registry
**Repro:** grep a project's operating docs for a number the canon re-anchored; compare against the function that computes it, on a case where the grid is non-uniform.
**Trigger:** the owner re-anchors or retires a rule → grep every document for the OLD formulation the same session, and add the doc↔code pair to the registry if the rule carries a number.
**Not for:** documents that quote a rule as HISTORY with its date and superseded marker — an original is not rewritten to match today's canon.

### EXP-0150 · 2026-08-25 · ❌→✅ · #mutation #state-leak #overwritten-evidence #close-the-class #rollback
**Context:** a phase added a SECOND kind of device state (a clock lock beside the curve) and released it on the existing rollback path. The mutation list was written before the work, and one of its addressees was the obvious one: «arm the lock and never release it».
**Tried / did:** ran it. The suite passed **51 of 51** — the mutation reddened NOTHING, while every rung of every band was leaving `-lgc` on the card.
**Result:** ✅ the zero was the finding. The leak was invisible **by construction**: the next rung OVERWRITES the lock with its own, so no intermediate state ever looks wrong, and nothing at the end of the band asked the card what still held it. Fixed by asking the card at rest (`peek().lock`), and — because the new state is written on EVERY band above the ceiling floor — the guard went on all five bands, not on the one trap whose subject it was. Re-run: 4 assertions red, each naming the leaked range («замок 180…2842 МГц»).
**Lesson:** **a state that the next iteration overwrites leaves no evidence of having leaked, so the only place it can be caught is AT REST — after the loop, by asking the device, not by counting your own calls.** The general shape is worth more than the fix: a rollback for state that is re-established every cycle is exercised constantly and verified never, because every intermediate observation shows a plausible value. Second half, and it is what turned a passing suite into a finding: **a mutation that reddens ZERO blocks is not a mutation that failed — it is a measurement that the coverage is absent**, and it is the cheapest such measurement there is. Treat a green mutation run as a red suite result.   → link: `plans/45` · `plans/44` · EXP-0148 (the neighbouring shape: an incidental catch mistaken for a direct check) · `BUG_FIXING_FRAMEWORK.md` → Guards
**Repro:** `npm run traps` after deleting the release of a per-iteration device state → 0 failures, because iteration N+1 rewrites what iteration N left.
**Trigger:** adding a device/global state that each loop iteration re-establishes → before trusting its rollback, write the AT-REST assertion first, then mutate the release away and demand red. Also: any mutation that reddens nothing → stop and ask what is not covered, never record it as «the code is robust».
**Not for:** state whose every write is independently asserted at the moment it happens — there the intermediate observation is the evidence and an at-rest check adds nothing.

### EXP-0149 · 2026-08-25 · ❌→✅ · #text-hygiene #line-endings #byte-exact-guards #shell-edit #invisible
**Context:** editing one block of prose in a long document. The project's canon says plainly: *"prose is edited with file tools, never through shell arguments"* — and the edit was a clean anchored replace, so a small script felt safe enough.
**Tried / did:** a Python read-modify-write with an exact anchor. The replacement landed correctly and `git diff` showed exactly the intended 29 insertions / 5 deletions — nothing else. The build gate nevertheless went red: a byte-exact guard reported the document's prayer block had diverged from its single source, though not one character of it had changed.
**Result:** ✅ caught by the guard, fixed by re-laying the block from its source. Cause: the file mixes line endings — the generated block is written with LF, the rest of the document is CRLF — and the round-trip through text mode normalised the whole file to one convention. `git diff` could not show it because git normalises line endings on comparison; only the byte-exact guard could see it.
**Lesson:** **a script that reads and rewrites a whole file rewrites parts you did not touch — invisibly, and in ways `git diff` is specifically built to hide.** Line endings, a trailing newline, a BOM: every one of them is invisible to the eye, invisible to review, and fatal to any byte-exact comparison. This is why the rule says FILE TOOLS, not "careful scripts": a targeted edit tool changes the bytes it is pointed at and nothing else, while a round-trip re-encodes everything. Second half, and it is the reassuring one: the guard that caught this exists because the project already paid for invisible text corruption once — and byte-exact guards are the ONLY instrument that sees this class, because the diff tool everyone trusts is designed to normalise exactly what went wrong.   → link: `AGENT_GUIDE.md` → Document & text hygiene · `GPU_TUNING_RAILS.md` STOP-line 7 · `tools/prayer.mjs` · EXP-0067 (encoding corruption with a green exit code)
**Repro:** read a mixed-ending file in text mode, write it back unchanged, run any byte-exact comparison against a single-source block inside it.
**Trigger:** about to edit prose with anything other than a file-editing tool → don't. If a script is genuinely unavoidable, run every byte-exact guard afterwards and never trust `git diff` alone to prove nothing else moved.
**Not for:** files the script OWNS and regenerates wholly (generated artifacts) — there a uniform re-encoding is the point, not a hazard.

### EXP-0148 · 2026-08-25 · ❌→✅ · #bench #fidelity #test-double #incidental-pass #trap-first
**Context:** a defect measured nine times on live hardware could not be reproduced offline at all — the test double honoured the constraint the real card broke, structurally (`if (f > ceiling) continue`). Phase 1 of the fix was therefore "teach the double to break it", before any repair.
**Tried / did:** built the trap from the measurement (the overshoot is always a whole number of grid steps: 2 in seven cases, 3 in two — so the trap takes the SMALLER), and proved the mechanism at zero descent depth so it could not be green for a neighbouring reason. Then found the double failed at a DIFFERENT site than the card: it stopped at "delivery above stock" instead of the ceiling check. Fixed that by clamping the double's voltage question the way the live atom asks it — and reverted the fix ten minutes later.
**Result:** ✅ the revert was the finding. Removing the wrong stop made the trap catch NOTHING (0 skipped, no holder) — proving the defect had only ever been caught INCIDENTALLY, by a neighbouring check, and that no direct check existed on the bench at all. The real gap turned out to be one level up: the bench atom never runs the delivered-clock judgement, because it has no sampler and the live judgement reads sampled clocks under load.
**Lesson:** **when a double fails at a different site than the real thing, do not move the double to the right site — ask whether the right site is exercised on the bench at all.** An incidental catch and a direct check are indistinguishable while both are red, and they diverge the moment you remove the incidental one. The cheapest way to tell them apart is exactly what happened here: remove the incidental path and see whether anything still fails — if nothing does, the coverage was never there. Second half: a trap must prove its MECHANISM under conditions where nothing else could produce the symptom (here: zero descent depth, so only the trap's own field can push the card above the ceiling) — otherwise a mutation removing the mechanism reddens nothing, which this project has already paid for twice.   → link: `plans/44` · `bugs/50` · `researches/11` §8 · EXP-0077
**Repro:** `npm run traps` with `T8_runs_above_the_ceiling_it_was_given`; then clamp `makeSweepStepFn`'s voltage question and watch the trap go from red-for-the-wrong-reason to green-for-no-reason.
**Trigger:** a bench reproduction fails at a different site than production → before relocating it, delete the site it DID fail at and check whether anything is left. Also: writing a trap → assert its mechanism under the one condition where nothing else can produce the symptom.
**Not for:** doubles whose divergence is cosmetic (message wording, ordering of equal-weight checks) — there the site does not carry meaning.

### EXP-0147 · 2026-08-25 · ❌→✅ · #guards #directions #twin-check #assumption-in-a-comment #data-loss
**Context:** a guard closed the previous day against "an interrupted descent overwrites a deeper measured value" held on the live path — three times in one run. The same run nevertheless raised 22 measured rows to a shallower voltage: the identical damage, the identical 22 frequencies, one day after the fix.
**Tried / did:** compared the two paths line by line instead of assuming a regression. The guard was intact and had fired correctly; it protects the row being WRITTEN. The damage came from the ratchet propagating that write UPWARD to other rows — a third direction the guard never covered, and whose header stated in writing: *"no separate guard is needed here: with no shallower write there is nothing to propagate."* The live run refuted the sentence — the written row had been at FACTORY, so the write was legal, and the propagation was the damage.
**Result:** ✅ fixed with the SAME predicate the other two directions already use, so no second definition of "demands its voltage" entered the module. The document was repaired surgically (22 raised rows restored, 13 legitimately deepened rows kept) and the next live run proved it: raised **0**, with the guard visibly firing three more times.
**Lesson:** **an assumption written into a comment is still an assumption, and the more confidently it is phrased the longer nobody measures it.** The dangerous form is not "we forgot this direction" — it is "we considered this direction and argued it away", because the argument becomes documentation and documentation is not re-checked. When a guard covers N of M directions, the honest artifact is a LIST of all M with each one's state, not prose explaining why the uncovered ones are safe. Corollary paid for here: enumerate a mechanism's directions mechanically (who writes, who propagates, who inherits) rather than from the narrative of the incident that prompted the fix — the incident shows one direction by construction.   → link: `bugs/57` · `bugs/55` · EXP-0144 (the same lesson one day earlier, one direction narrower)
**Repro:** close an UNTOUCHED row with a `cut-short` status at a voltage deeper than its stock but shallower than what is measured above it → `raised` non-empty before the fix, empty after.
**Trigger:** installed or inherited a guard for a class → list every direction the mechanism can act in, and mark each covered/uncovered by RUNNING it, never by reasoning. A comment claiming a direction is safe is the first place to point a mutation at.
**Not for:** mechanisms with a single write path and no propagation — there the direction list is one line and the ceremony is not worth it.

### EXP-0146 · 2026-08-25 · ❌→✅ · #evidence #instruments #unread-output #live-run #cheapest-first
**Context:** a bug whose document carried three ranked hypotheses and a plan whose first step was «run it live again». An entire epic phase had been built the day before for the sole purpose of making that bug legible — a write-failure classifier, a settle contract, new journal fields — and two live runs had gone by since.
**Tried / did:** before proposing any run, read what the instrument had ALREADY written. One command over the production journal: the field existed on 31 verdict lines and carried a value on exactly one. That single line, plus the six lines of the last band, killed the document's leading hypothesis outright — `writeSettled: true`, point-by-point re-read green, and the curve after the write sitting EXACTLY on the ceiling — and split one bug into two failures that are repaired in different places.
**Result:** ✅ the investigation advanced further from reading than the two live runs had advanced it by running, at a cost of zero GPU writes, zero machine risk and about ten minutes. The proposed live run changed shape completely: from «repeat the band» (which would have re-confirmed a known answer) to «a band never swept», with the dry run's numbers to justify it.
**Lesson:** **an instrument built for a question keeps producing evidence after the session that built it ends, and the next session's first move is to READ IT — not to commission a new experiment.** The trap is structural, not careless: the session that builds an instrument writes «the answer comes from the next live run» into the plan, and the session that HAS that run reads the plan, not the output. Nobody is assigned the reading. The cheapest observation in any project is the one already on disk, and it outranks the most careful hypothesis — three ranked hypotheses died to one `grep` over a file that had been sitting there for nine hours.   → link: `bugs/50` · `plans/40` (the instrument) · EXP-0141 (the same class one floor down: a field was passed and silently never landed)
**Repro:** `node -e` over `runs/sweep/journal.jsonl`, printing every row that carries the field the previous phase added — before writing a single line of new code.
**Trigger:** picking up a bug whose plan says «the answer comes from the next run» → first ask what the LAST run already recorded, and grep for the fields the fix added. Also: closing a phase that adds a diagnostic field → name in the handoff WHO reads it and WITH WHAT command, or the field is written for nobody.
**Not for:** questions the instrument genuinely cannot have answered yet (the field did not exist during any completed run) — there the reading is a two-minute check, not a substitute.

### EXP-0145 · 2026-08-25 · ❌ · #owner-machine #observation #proxy #no-sensor #showing
**Context:** the owner asked to be shown the run. The watch window was raised; its server reported «ОКНО: подключилось — прибор его видит», and that line was relayed to him in chat as fact. His reply: *«я не знал, что ты это показываешь. У меня давно всё закрыто»* — the whole live run had gone by with nobody watching.
**Tried / did:** the instrument was not naive — an earlier fix had already replaced «we spawned a browser» with «an event stream is connected», which is a genuine observation. What nobody asked was WHOSE stream. The same run's log named the likely answer two lines above: *«порт держит дашборд прошлой сессии (pid 14864)»* — a leftover browser process from an earlier session had reconnected.
**Result:** ❌ a run the owner explicitly wanted to watch was performed unobserved, and the agent's report asserted the opposite.
**Lesson:** **an instrument that measures a PROXY reports the proxy, and the honest sentence names it: not «you can see it» but «a stream is connected — do you see it?».** The failure survives a careful fix because the fix improves the proxy (spawn → connection) without changing its KIND: both answer «is something attached?», neither answers «is a human looking?». The tell is ownable in one question — *whose observation is this, and could it be true while the human sees nothing?* Second half, and it is the agent's half: a report about a surface you have NO SENSOR FOR is the false-`[TESTED]` fraud in the one place no judge can catch it — the human's screen. Relaying a tool's line verbatim does not transfer the claim's burden to the tool.   → link: `bugs/56` · `AGENT_GUIDE.md` → «NEVER SAY FIXED WHERE THE OBSERVATION IS NOT AVAILABLE TO YOU» · EXP-0125
**Repro:** open the page, close the TAB without killing the browser process, re-raise the dashboard → «подключилось» with an empty screen.
**Trigger:** about to tell a human that something is visible / open / shown → name the sensor you actually read. If the sensor is not their eyes, ask instead of asserting.
**Not for:** artifacts you produced and can read back yourself (a file, a rendered page's bytes) — there your sensor IS the truth.

### EXP-0144 · 2026-08-25 · ❌→✅ · #evidence #ratchet #strong-vs-weak #live-run #data-loss
**Context:** the first live acceptance run of a new document-writing path. The band gained 7 deeper rows — and 22 rows ABOVE it were pushed UP from 800–845 mV to 850, every one of them carrying a passed burn.
**Tried / did:** traced it instead of accepting the summary. A frequency whose descent was CUT SHORT by an unrelated defect wrote its last-passed voltage (850) over a deeper value already in the row (800), and the monotonicity ratchet then propagated 850 to every row above. The project already owned a guard for exactly this — «only a neighbour that PROVED its need with a FAILURE may raise» — and it was installed on one of the two ratchet directions.
**Result:** ✅ fixed at the source: a close whose status means «the descent STOPPED» (never «the frequency FAILED») may not write a shallower value over a deeper measured one. Replaying the exact offending write against the real snapshot: 22 rows raised → **0**, and the band still continues (the refusal is `ok: true`, not an abort).
**Lesson:** **a record that says «this worked» is not evidence that anything else does NOT work, and the moment you let it overwrite a stronger record, one interrupted step rewrites everything downstream of it.** Rank writes by what was OBSERVED, not by what is newest: an observed failure outranks an inherited pass, and a mere stop outranks nothing at all. Second half, worth more than the fix: **when a guard already exists for the class, check every direction it should cover** — the comment on this project's guard even narrated the identical incident from three days earlier, and it still guarded one side of two. A guard installed on one path is a guard that documents the defect it fails to prevent.   → link: `bugs/55` · `curve-store.closePoint` · `demandsVoltage`
**Repro:** `node tools/harvest-acceptance.mjs runs/measured.before-session49.json` against the pre-fix document → `строк ПОДНЯТО : 22`.
**Trigger:** any write that reconciles two records of the same fact → ask which one OBSERVED more, and make the weaker one refuse rather than the newer one win. Also: found a guard for a class? grep for every site of that class before calling it guarded.
**Not for:** stores with a single author and no history, where «newest wins» is the whole contract.

### EXP-0143 · 2026-08-24 · ❌→✅ · #simplicity #guards #scaffolding #owner-feedback #record-builders
**Context:** a record builder listed its fields explicitly and therefore dropped, in silence, any field its caller passed that the list did not name. Measured on the production store: **678 rows carrying half a measurement, the other half in zero of them.**
**Tried / did:** fixed the two fields, then built a guard for the CLASS — a field-name list plus a self-test block that PARSED the project's sources, collected the keys every caller passes, and compared them to the list. It needed comment-stripping and string-literal-stripping, and its first run produced a false finding (`T23`, a fragment of the timestamp `'2026-08-22T23:00:10+03:00'`), so the next fix went into the guard itself. The owner, watching: *«стенд должен становиться проще и гениальнее в своей простоте. KISS, Паретто»*.
**Result:** ✅ the enumeration was DELETED instead of guarded — the builder now spreads the caller's object, with the same names kept as DEFAULTS. Losing a field became impossible by construction, and the list, the guard, its parser, its stripping and its mutation all disappeared with it. The identical form was already in a neighbouring module of the same project, and the twin check had reported that module clean BY CONSTRUCTION — the simple answer was sitting in the evidence I had already collected.
**Lesson:** **when you catch yourself writing a guard over a mechanism, ask first whether the MECHANISM can be removed — a guarded hazard still costs a parser, a fixture, a mutation and a false-positive budget, while a removed hazard costs nothing forever.** The tell is precise and early: *your guard needed its own fix before its first green.* A guard that must be debugged is guarding the wrong thing. Second half, and it is what makes this findable next time: **the twin check's "clean" column is a catalogue of the simple form.** When one site out of five has the defect, the other four are not merely innocent — they are showing you the shape that cannot fail, and the fix is to make the fifth look like them rather than to watch it.   → link: `bugs/54` · `sweep-journal.writeVerdict` · `remembered-state.appendBootJournal` · `PHILOSOPHY.md` → Occam
**Repro:** `grep -n "\.\.\." <the builder>` — a builder that never spreads its argument drops whatever it does not name; diff the caller's keys against the record's keys and the loss is countable.
**Trigger:** about to add a guard, a linter or a checked list to keep two field-sets in step → ask whether one side can simply CONTAIN the other. Also: any explicit record builder (`{a: x.a, b: x.b, …}`) that persists data — check what its callers pass that it does not name.
**Not for:** boundaries where filtering IS the job — a public API surface, a redaction step, an untrusted caller. There the enumeration is the feature and the guard belongs on it.

### EXP-0142 · 2026-08-24 · ❌→✅ · #guards #product-over-process #exit-codes #scaffolding #owner-feedback
**Context:** the owner, after watching run after run end with **0 tuned frequencies**: *«Ты не продукт делаешь, а леса — это невероятно плохо. Рабочий продукт важнее процессов. Здание — важнее лесов.»* · *«Сторож должен не ронять инструмент, а подсказывать инструменту, что пошло не так… Он должен писать логи, А ТЫ ПО ЛОГАМ УЖЕ ПОНИМАЕШЬ, где в продукте есть косяки.»*
**Tried / did:** every guard in the engine was individually justified and every one of them, on firing, killed the WHOLE run. Two evenings: 0 of 7, then 0 of 27 — one anomaly on one rung threw away work already paid for in minutes of burning on the owner's hardware. The remedy was NOT to weaken any guard. It was to notice that two different questions had been answered by one mechanism.
**Result:** ✅ separated **«may this enter the record?»** (answer unchanged: no unverified value ever) from **«may the work continue?»** (answer changed: yes — close that item with its named cause and move on). Coverage went 0 → 4 of 25 on the next run, with every skip carrying its class and the address of the code that gave up. Kept exactly three barriers, all about the MACHINE, not about strictness.
**Lesson:** **a guard that halts the whole job on a local fault is not strict — it is confused about scope, and it converts a product into scaffolding.** The tell is the owner's number, not the code review: if run after run ends with zero delivered units while every individual check is defensible, the checks are answering the wrong question. Split it: *recording* stays as strict as it was; *continuing* becomes the default, with the fault NAMED, LOGGED, and carrying a stable identifier of the code site that could not cope — so the log becomes the repair queue rather than an obituary. **Second half, and it is what keeps the split honest:** make the exit code report whether the job PRODUCED anything, not whether anything went wrong. Otherwise the fix swings to the opposite lie — a run that delivered nothing reporting success.   → link: `GOAL.md` → «🏗 ЗДАНИЕ ВАЖНЕЕ ЛЕСОВ» · `engine.mjs` → `report.skipped` · `plans/41`
**Repro:** count delivered units per run across the last N runs. A long string of zeros with a green test suite IS the symptom.
**Trigger:** any run/pipeline/batch whose guards abort the whole job → ask of each: does this fault make the REMAINING items unsafe, or only this one? Only the first may halt.
**Not for:** faults that genuinely invalidate the rest — an unknown machine state, a resource that cannot be described, a corrupted shared artifact. Those must still stop everything, and naming which ones is part of the fix.

### EXP-0141 · 2026-08-24 · ❌→✅ · #evidence #harvest #measurement #half-a-pair #persistence
**Context:** the owner asked whether the knowledge from each burn settles anywhere: *«карта в конце-то концов на какой-то конкретной паре частота/напряжение работала — это знание оседает в виде пользы прогона? или мы это выбрасываем?»*
**Tried / did:** checked the code rather than answering from memory. The delivered frequency IS measured every rung (median of the loaded telemetry samples) and the serving voltage IS read back from the card. But the journal's verdict line carried the voltage and **not** the frequency — and only the LAST rung of a descent ever reached the document. One run: 23 rungs, **16 held a burn, 4 rows written**.
**Result:** ✅ the missing half now persists per rung; harvesting the rest is planned as its own epic.
**Lesson:** **a measurement persisted by halves is a measurement lost, and the loss is invisible because the surviving half looks like a record.** The voltage column was full, so nothing looked broken. The test is not «do we record it?» but **«can a later session RECONSTRUCT the claim from what is on disk?»** — here it could not, and could never: the median is computed over samples of that burn and does not exist anywhere afterwards. Corollary about intermediate results: when a process has a headline output (the edge) and by-products (every rung that passed), the by-products are usually dropped by construction, not by decision — nobody ever chose to discard them. Ask what the expensive step produced BESIDES its headline.   → link: `plans/41` · `ideas/12` · `GOAL.md` → «🎚 ТЮНИМ ТО, ЧТО КАРТА ВЫДАЁТ»
**Repro:** `node -e` over the journal: count verdict lines with `outcome === 'passed'` that carry `servingMvAfter` but no `deliveredMhz`.
**Trigger:** any expensive measurement → list what it produced, then grep the store for EACH field. A field measured and not stored is the default outcome, not an anomaly.
**Not for:** by-products that are genuinely derivable later from what IS stored — there storing them twice is the defect.

### EXP-0140 · 2026-08-24 · ❌→✅ · #classification #evidence #fixtures #diagnosis #surfaces
**Context:** naming which of six modelled ways a curve write had failed. Five classes were recognisable from the control structure (what we wrote vs what the card holds); the sixth — «the driver adjusted the result» — refused to be.
**Tried / did:** the bench's C3 fixture nudges the first pressed-down entry by +15 MHz. That entry's own offset happens to be −15, so the adjusted value lands on **exactly zero** — which in the control structure is byte-identical to an entry that was never written at all. The classifier answered «partly inert», and it was RIGHT to: from that surface the two are the same observation. The tempting fix was one character in the fixture's nudge.
**Result:** ✅ the fixture was left untouched and the classifier was given the evidence the class is actually **defined** by — the effective curve's top above the ceiling, which no arithmetic of ours can produce. A caller that cannot measure it passes `null` and gets the honest lesser diagnosis.
**Lesson:** **when a class refuses to be recognised, ask which SURFACE its signature lives on before touching either the fixture or the threshold.** A classifier reading surface A cannot name a class defined on surface B, and every remedy that stays on A is a fudge: bending the fixture makes the suite green about a case that no longer exists; loosening a threshold makes the neighbouring classes wrong. The honest move is to carry the second surface's number into the decision — and, where the caller genuinely cannot measure it, to return the weaker true answer rather than the stronger invented one. **The by-product is worth more than the fix:** «this evidence alone cannot settle that hypothesis» is a requirement for the NEXT measurement, and it only becomes visible when you refuse to fudge.   → link: `plans/40` · `automation-engine/lib/nvapi.mjs` → `classifyWriteFailure` · `researches/18` §5
**Repro:** classify the bench's C3 fixture with and without `offeredAboveCapMhz` — `C2` and `C3` off the same table; both blocks are in `vgpu --selftest`.
**Trigger:** a fixture that will not produce the class it models, or two classes whose evidence is identical → name the surface each class is DEFINED on before changing anything.
**Not for:** a class that is genuinely a sub-case of another — there merging is right and a second surface is ceremony.

### EXP-0139 · 2026-08-24 · ❌→✅ · #settling #read-back #polling #stale-data #owner-machine
**Context:** the canon's rule for any state-changing write is «read until two consecutive samples agree» (EXP-0014, measured 2026-08-10). Applying it to the curve write, on the bench that models an unsettled read.
**Tried / did:** wired the existing agreement-based helper into the write path. The bench models the class faithfully — «the first N reads return the PRE-WRITE table» — and the contract accepted the stale table on probe 2: **the two stale probes agree with each other perfectly.**
**Result:** ✅ the poll was rewritten to wait for the card to hold **what we asked**, not for quiet. Fast path costs nothing (an obedient card answers on probe 1, no sleep); on expiry the last two probes tell «still moving» (the unsettled class) from «stopped somewhere else» (classified by shape). The probe COUNT is now reported on success too — `probes > 1` means the class happened and was waited out.
**Lesson:** **«wait for quiet» and «wait for the write» are different contracts, and quiet is the weaker one exactly where it matters: a stale value is perfectly stable.** Agreement is the right contract only for state we did NOT author and therefore cannot predict — a released clock, a ramping fan. Where we know the target, poll for the target; the fast path gets cheaper, not more expensive, and the bound's expiry becomes an ANSWER (two distinguishable ones) instead of a shrug. Corollary that cost nothing and pays every run: **record how many probes the wait took even when it succeeded** — that number is the direct evidence for whether the phenomenon is happening at all.   → link: `automation-engine/lib/nvapi.mjs` → `pollUntilApplied` · `plans/40` · `AGENT_GUIDE.md` → the owner's-machine rule, step 4
**Repro:** `node automation-engine/lib/nvapi.mjs --selftest-shape` → block «ДВЕ СОВПАВШИЕ УСТАРЕВШИЕ ПРОБЫ — НЕ ОТВЕТ»; mutation NC (restore the agreement contract) reddens it alone.
**Trigger:** writing a read-back/settle loop → ask whether you can name the value you expect. If yes, poll for it; agreement is the fallback for states you cannot predict.
**Not for:** reads of state nobody wrote (idle telemetry, a wandering clock after release) — there agreement is the only contract available.

### EXP-0138 · 2026-08-24 · ❌→✅ · #doubles #bench #tautology #simulation #blind-green
**Context:** the live sweep kept failing while the whole offline battery stayed green. Went looking for why the bench could not reproduce a defect the card produced twice a day.
**Tried / did:** read the double's write path instead of trusting its reputation. `virtual-gpu.mjs` answered a curve write by calling `buildRaiseAndCapVector` — **the same function that computed the write** — and storing its output as the card's state. Then read the bench's substitute atom: it returned a verdict and a delivered clock, and performed **none** of the live atom's checks (no post-write curve read, no point-by-point verification, no ceiling block).
**Result:** ✅ inverted it: the card holds its own table, a write mutates that table through an injectable seam, every read is derived from the table. Within the hour the bench moved a hypothesis: the leading suspect predicted a FACTORY top on the read-back, the live card had shown cap+15, and a different class produced exactly cap+15.
**Lesson:** **a double that computes its answer with the producer's own function is not a test, it is a tautology wearing a test's clothes — and it goes green hardest exactly where the producer is wrong.** The tell is structural and greppable, not behavioural: *does the double IMPORT anything from the module under test to compute its RESPONSE?* Inputs may be shared (grids, fixtures, refusals — a double that refuses less than the card is its own defect); the ANSWER may not. Second half, worth as much: a double is only as good as the CHECKS run against it — ours modelled the card's state and the harness never asked it the three questions the live path asks, so the state would not have been read even once it existed. Check both halves: what the double computes, and what the caller bothers to ask it.   → link: `researches/18` §6 · `plans/38` · `bugs/50`
**Repro:** `grep -n "<the module under test>" <the double>.mjs` — every hit must be an INPUT (a shared refusal, a shared grid) or a comment. A hit inside the double's response path is the tautology.
**Trigger:** building or trusting a simulator/mock/virtual device → grep it for imports from the module it is supposed to judge, and diff the questions the harness asks it against the questions the real path asks.
**Not for:** doubles that only STAND IN for slowness (a fake clock, a stub network) and make no claim about correctness — there sharing arithmetic costs nothing.

### EXP-0137 · 2026-08-24 · ❌→✅ · #json #serialization #silent-loss #journal #evidence
**Context:** adding the first NESTED field to the write-ahead journal's verdict line (an array of the atom's red blocks, each `{name, detail}`).
**Tried / did:** the block asserted the names came back. They came back `null` — the array had the right LENGTH and every element was `{}`.
**Result:** ✅ the cause is `JSON.stringify(record, Object.keys(record).sort())`, used to get a stable field order. An ARRAY second argument is not a sort — it is a **key filter, applied at EVERY level of the document**, so a nested object keeps only the keys that happen to exist at the TOP level and loses the rest with no error. Replaced by building a key-sorted object and stringifying that.
**Lesson:** **`JSON.stringify`'s second argument as an array is a whitelist, not an ordering — and it silently guts nested objects.** Sorting keys is what people want; filtering the document is what they get. The wider rule this instance belongs to: a serializer defect loses data with a GREEN exit code, so it is invisible until someone reads the output for a purpose the original author did not have — here, four years of flat journal lines hid an armed trap that fired on the first diagnostic field anyone nested. When a store's shape widens, assert the ROUND TRIP of the new shape, not just that the write returned.   → link: `automation-engine/lib/sweep-journal.mjs` → `appendLine` · `plans/37`
**Repro:** `node -e "console.log(JSON.stringify({a:1,b:{c:2}}, ['a','b']))"` → `{"a":1,"b":{}}` — the nested `c` is gone, no error.
**Trigger:** adding a nested object/array to any record that a serializer writes → assert the round trip of the NESTED value, not the top-level field.
**Not for:** the function form of the replacer (`(k,v)=>…`), which transforms rather than filters.

### EXP-0136 · 2026-08-24 · ❌→✅ · #twin-check #inventory #parameters #journal #evidence
**Context:** closing `bugs/49` — a fix (`bugs/47`) had taught the atom to RECOMPUTE its caller's offset, and the write-ahead journal kept recording the caller's number, so the evidence about a hardware write no longer described the hardware write.
**Tried / did:** the fix itself was one shape (the intent stays a CLAIM because R15 forbids moving it after the write; the verdict appends the FACT). Then the twin check — but not by grepping the field name. Grepped for **assignments to the function's own parameters** inside the atom: `awk 'NR>=629 && NR<=1230' vf-step.mjs | grep -E '^\s{0,6}(param|names)\s*='`.
**Result:** ✅ TWO reassignments, not one. `offsetMhz = aim.offsetMhz` (the known defect) and `pinMhz = snap.mhz` — the pin snapped onto the card's own clock ladder, about which the journal recorded **nothing at all**. Below the cap floor the sweep does pin, so a snap that ever moves the clock makes the line lie in `frequencyMhz` — the field the whole journal is keyed by.
**Lesson:** **when a callee OVERRIDES a caller's number, the twin is found by searching for PARAMETER REASSIGNMENT, not for the field's name.** A name search finds the places that already talk about the quantity; the defect lives exactly where nobody talks about it. Second half, about what to leave behind: the second twin's danger could not be settled offline (does the snap ever move on the sweep's path? the grids *should* coincide), and «should» is a guess. **Recording the number is strictly better than filing a note** — a field turns an unanswerable question into a measurement the next live run takes for free, while a note waits for someone to remember it. Third: `null` and `0` are DIFFERENT answers in an evidence store — «not measured» vs «measured, and they agreed» — and collapsing them writes an observation nobody made.   → link: `bugs/49` · `bugs/47` · R15 · `sweep-journal.writeVerdict`
**Repro:** `node automation-engine/engine.mjs --selftest 2>&1 | grep 'bugs/49'` — four blocks. To see the net work, change `record.appliedDeltaMhz` to `plan.deltaMhz` in `close()` and watch the first block go red naming the engine's number.
**Trigger:** teaching a callee to recompute or snap something its caller passed in → grep the callee for ALL parameter reassignments, then check every audit trail the caller writes.
**Not for:** a callee that only READS its arguments — there caller and card cannot disagree, and the search returns nothing by construction.

### EXP-0135 · 2026-08-24 · ❌ · #bug-documents #stale-plan #already-built #two-strikes
**Context:** picking up `bugs/48` («a deliberate stop is recorded as a machine hang»). Its plan named four steps; steps 1 and 2 were «build an `operator-stop` outcome class» and «make the signal handler write a verdict before exiting».
**Tried / did:** before building either, grepped for them: `grep -rn 'closeAsOperatorStop\|operator-stop' automation-engine tools`.
**Result:** ❌ both had existed since 2026-08-16 (`bugs/14`), plus a ready CLI (`tools/close-operator-stop.mjs`). The document's claim «`SIGINT`/`SIGTERM` call `process.exit(130)`, so no verdict appears» described the code as it was BEFORE that fix. The real cause of the incident was that the run had been killed **hard** (`TaskStop`, no signal) — a path that by design reaches no handler — and the existing tool was never run afterwards.
**Lesson:** **a bug document is written under time pressure by a session that is losing context, so its PLAN is a hypothesis about the code, not a reading of it — verify each step exists-or-not before building it.** The tell is specific and cheap to check: a plan step that describes current behaviour in the present tense («сейчас X делает Y») is an assertion about code, and assertions about code are grep-able in one command. Second half, and it is the expensive one: `EXPERIENCE.md` ALREADY carried this exact case («a hard stop is not finished until the journal is closed by hand»), the tool already existed, and the step still was not taken. By the journal's own two-strikes rule that lesson has failed as text and owes a mechanism — a stop path that cannot be forgotten, not a third reminder.   → link: `bugs/48` · `bugs/14` · `tools/close-operator-stop.mjs`
**Repro:** before executing any numbered step of a bug's «план починки», run `grep -rn '<the thing the step says to build>' automation-engine tools` and read the result. A hit means the step is stale — correct the document in place before touching code.
**Trigger:** opening a bug document written by an earlier session → grep every plan step that asserts what the code «currently» does, BEFORE the first edit.
**Not for:** a bug you diagnosed yourself in this same session — there the plan and the code were read minutes apart.

### EXP-0134 · 2026-08-24 · ❌→✅ · #vacuous-assertion #self-reading-block #mutation #testing
**Context:** `plans/30` §2.6 — the logon disarm rides an injectable seam (`disarmDisksFn`). Fixture blocks prove the duty; **nothing proved the PRODUCTION call site passes the seam** — the EXP-0133 hole exactly. So I wrote a block that reads this module's OWN SOURCE and asserts the `--boot-apply` branch contains `disarmDisksFn:`.
**Tried / did:** anchored it with `src.indexOf("argv.includes('--boot-apply')")`. Green. Then the mutation: delete the seam from the production branch.
**Result:** ❌ **still green.** The anchor matched the BLOCK'S OWN SOURCE LINE (the block contains that string), and `disarmDisksFn:` was found in the block's own refusal message. A guard reporting on protection that did not exist. Re-anchored on the branch's printed line — **green again on a broken tree**, for the same reason one level up: the block now contained THAT string too. Fixed by (a) building the search key from concatenated parts so it never appears whole in the block's own source, (b) `lastIndexOf`, and (c) a tripwire that fails if the window contains `block(`.
**Lesson:** **a block that reads its own file must not contain its own search key literally** — `indexOf` finds the block before it finds the subject, and self-match makes it vacuously green forever. Build the key from parts, prefer `lastIndexOf` when the subject sits after the suite, and add a tripwire that detects «I found myself». More generally: source-reading assertions are the one family where the mutation is not optional — a normal block fails loudly when miswired; this kind succeeds.   → link: EXP-0133 · plans/30_safe_mode_for_the_owners_disks.md
**Repro:** break the production line the block guards → it MUST redden. If it stays green, print the anchor's line number: `src.slice(0,at).split('\n').length`.
**Trigger:** any assertion that greps the repo's own source (wiring guards, «is the seam passed», «is the flag still there»).
**Not for:** blocks over runtime values — those cannot match themselves.

### EXP-0133 · 2026-08-23 · ❌→✅ · #mutation #wiring #unit-vs-endtoend #EXP-0052 #testing
**Context:** epic 33 phase 2 — a new tag had to travel from a pure decision function, through `closePoint`, into the curve document's row. Unit blocks covered `overshootMarkFor` (does it mark?) and `closePoint` (does it accept the tag and refuse a fake one?). Both green, six blocks.
**Tried / did:** ran mutation N3 — delete the one line that PASSES the tag from the engine to `closePoint`. It applied (−45 bytes, asserted per EXP-0132).
**Result:** ❌ **nothing reddened.** Both ends were guarded and the wire between them was not. Fixed with an end-to-end block through `sweepRange` on a fixture card that serves 15 mV above every order, asserting on the DOCUMENT ROW. N3 then reddened 2 blocks. A follow-up mutation (N5, swap the tag for another valid one) exposed a second weakness: the witness block read `marked[0].provenBy`, so it fell over together with the tag and could not tell «the tag didn't travel» from «the number wasn't named» — the witness now searches all rows independently.
**Lesson:** **guarding both ENDS of a wire is not guarding the wire.** Unit blocks on a producer and a consumer both stay green while the call between them is deleted. Every fact that must reach an artifact needs one block that reads it OFF THE ARTIFACT. This is EXP-0052 in a new costume («граница, добавленная в ПРОГОН, не добавлена, пока её не печатает ПЛАН») — the same defect keeps arriving from a different direction, which is why it is worth a second entry. Corollary: when two facts ride the same wire, assert them INDEPENDENTLY, or one mutation masks the other.   → link: plans/35_epic33_phase2_the_overshoot_mark.md · EXP-0052
**Repro:** for any new field/tag reaching a document: delete the line that passes it at the call site → at least one block must redden. If none does, the end-to-end block is missing.
**Trigger:** adding anything that must land in `curves/measured.json` or any other artifact → write the block that reads it back out, not only the blocks on the functions.
**Not for:** purely internal computations that never leave the process — unit blocks are the whole story there.

### EXP-0132 · 2026-08-23 · ❌→✅ · #mutation #false-green #crlf #windows #testing
**Context:** proving phase 1 of epic 33 — five mutations, each expected to redden only its own blocks. Four reddened. **M3 came back «63 зелёных, 0 красных», i.e. the guard did not notice its own subject being deleted.**
**Tried / did:** almost wrote it up as a blind block. Checked instead whether the mutation had applied at all: the file has **CRLF** line terminators, my `replace()` anchor ended in `\n`, so the string never matched and `writeFileSync` wrote the file back UNCHANGED. Re-ran with `\r\n` — the block reddened immediately and alone.
**Result:** ✅ M3 proven; the block was never blind. ❌ the harness was — for one silent minute it reported a healthy guard on a mutation that never existed.
**Lesson:** **a mutation that fails to apply is indistinguishable from a guard that passes** — and it is the more dangerous of the two, because green is the answer you were hoping for. A mutation harness must ASSERT that the file changed (anchor found · byte count moved), never infer it from the test result. On this machine that means multi-line anchors need `\r\n`: every source file here is CRLF.   → link: plans/34_epic33_phase1_the_sixth_stop_tag.md · TESTING_FRAMEWORK.md
**Repro:** `node -e "const s=require('fs').readFileSync(f,'utf8'); if(!s.includes(anchor)) {console.log('ЯКОРЬ НЕ НАЙДЕН'); process.exit(1)}"` before every mutation; print `before.length - after.length`.
**Trigger:** any mutation testing on this repo → single-line anchors, or `\r\n`, and always a «did it apply» assertion first.
**Not for:** single-line anchors with no newline in them — those matched fine (M1 · M2 · M4 · M5 all applied).

### EXP-0131 · 2026-08-23 · ❌→✅ · #stamps #EXP-0019 #two-strikes #mechanize #heartbeat
**Context:** the guarded loop's heartbeat line is written by hand, timestamp included; in one session the agent typed `18:44` when the clock said 18:20 and `18:40` when it said 18:32 — while every OTHER number in the same session was measured by a tool.
**Tried / did:** did not add a third reminder. The one field a reader cannot verify by looking became the one field nobody types: `tools/heartbeat.mjs` composes the line and takes the stamp from the machine's clock (`--file` for Russian text, argv is the wrong pipe for it).
**Result:** ✅ the tool's stamp matched `new Date()` to the second on the proving run; the two wrong lines were corrected from the clock and from the commit receipts (`git log --date=format`).
**Lesson:** **the tell for «mechanize now» is not the size of the error, it is WHERE the error lives — in the one field the writer cannot check by reading it back.** An agent re-reads its own prose and sees prose; it re-reads its own timestamp and sees a plausible number. Any field like that (a stamp, a count, a duration) is written by a tool the moment it is wrong twice.   → link: EXP-0019 · EXP-0012 · tools/heartbeat.mjs
**Repro:** `node tools/heartbeat.mjs --file <one-line note>` — then compare the appended stamp with `node -e "console.log(new Date().toString())"`.
**Trigger:** about to type a time, a date or a count into a document → take it from the tool that owns it (the clock, the battery's own output line, `git log`), never from the head.
**Not for:** an approximate stamp that is HONESTLY approximate (`≈ 10:05`) — the canon allows it and the tool does not replace it.

### EXP-0130 · 2026-08-23 · ❌→✅ · #guards #reachability #neighbouring-block #do-not-delete
**Context:** a new gate refused to raise the review page over an unanswerable document; a neighbouring QA block (B4) had asserted its property by driving the server on exactly such a document, and went red.
**Tried / did:** did NOT delete or relax B4, and did not add a bypass for it. Asked what B4 actually guards — «the writer's report matches what landed» — and drove that property one floor down, at the library call, where it still lives.
**Result:** ✅ both guarantees kept: the gate protects the HUMAN from an unanswerable page, B4 protects a LIBRARY CALLER from a lying report. 19 blocks, 0 failed.
**Lesson:** **a new guard that reddens a neighbouring block is a reachability question, not a correctness one.** Ask which layer the old block's property actually lives on; a property whose front door you just closed usually still holds one floor down. Deleting the block loses a guarantee nobody decided to give up, and a bypass flag for the suite is the hatch through which the closed class returns.   → link: bugs/41 · tools/verify-review-contour.mjs
**Repro:** `npm run verify:contour` — blocks `ANSWERABLE` and `B4` must BOTH be green; force `answerabilityRefusals` to return `[]` and only `ANSWERABLE` reddens.
**Trigger:** a new gate makes an existing test's scenario unreachable → move the assertion to the layer that still owns the property, never delete it and never add a test-only bypass.
**Not for:** a block whose property genuinely ceased to exist (the feature was removed) — that one is deleted, with the reason written in the commit.

### EXP-0129 · 2026-08-23 · ❌→✅ · #owner #guards #truthful-refusal #wrong-addressee
**Context:** the owner answered two interviews through the review contour and NEITHER answer reached its document — one had no `**Ответ:**` fields, the other no NUMBERED question heading, so the page gave him no input at all («поле для ответа было не доступно для ввода»).
**Tried / did:** the machinery was already honest — it refused by name and said exactly why, and had done so since `bugs/01` → B4. The fix was not more honesty but a change of ADDRESSEE: the same two checks now run when the page is RAISED, so the refusal reaches the agent before the owner is called, with exit code 2 and no `--force`.
**Result:** ✅ guard red on both real documents, then green after repair; a mutation reddens its own block.
**Lesson:** **a truthful refusal delivered to the wrong person is still a defect.** When a tool reports «I could not do X» to a HUMAN who is standing in front of it, ask what would have had to run EARLIER for that same sentence to reach the person who can fix it. Honesty of a message and usefulness of a message are different properties, and a project that has fixed the first can still be failing the second for years.   → link: bugs/41 · interviews/011 · interviews/012
**Repro:** `node tools/review.mjs <interview.md> --no-serve --no-signal --no-open` on a document whose question heading carries no number → exit 2 and «СТРАНИЦА НЕ ПОДНЯТА».
**Trigger:** writing any tool that reports «could not» at the moment a human is already waiting → move the check to the moment the human is INVITED.
**Not for:** checks that genuinely cannot run early (they depend on what the human types) — those stay where they are and keep reporting honestly.

### EXP-0128 · 2026-08-23 · ❌→✅ · #guards #trust #false-diagnosis #retired-tool #verify-the-verdict
**Context:** `STATUS.md` had carried, for six weeks, the sentence that a mechanical guard «краснеет всегда, все находки ложные» — so the rule it enforces was left to the agent's attention instead, and had already been broken once.
**Tried / did:** ran the tool and OPENED all nine of its findings one by one instead of trusting either the tool or the sentence about it.
**Result:** ❌ the sentence was false — eight findings were false positives but ONE WAS REAL: three unanswered questions to the owner, in a research document, since the previous day, on a taste-class subject only his ear can settle. ✅ closed that one (they now live in `interviews/`), then fixed the largest mechanical false class; the guard went 9 → 8 → 6, each step witnessed by re-running it. And the ratio was never even bad: the canon's own expectation for a text-rule guard is «~10 false hits per real one», so at 8:1 the tool was INSIDE spec when it was written off.
**Lesson:** **a verdict ABOUT an instrument is a claim like any other, and it decays faster than the instrument does.** «It always cries wolf» is the cheapest sentence in a project and the most expensive: it costs nothing to write, it is never re-measured, and from the moment it is written every true finding the tool produces is invisible. The test that catches it takes minutes — open every finding, classify each by hand, count. Second half, about method: **a noisy guard is fixed by SCOPE, not by suppression.** The chronicle of the closed past was scoped out (a question recorded there is a fact, not a queue, and the one action the guard could provoke is the one the chronicle forbids) — the same shape this project already used for stamps: scope needs no list to keep up to date, a suppression file does.
**Repro:** for any tool the project calls «broken», run it and classify EVERY finding by hand before believing the label; compare the false:real ratio against the canon's stated expectation for that kind of guard.
**Trigger:** meeting a sentence like «this check is unreliable / always red / all its findings are false» in the project's own documents.
**Not for:** a tool measured broken WITH the classification attached — there the work is done and the verdict is evidence, not hearsay.

### EXP-0127 · 2026-08-23 · ❌→✅ · #guards #mutation #diagnosis #red-line
**Context:** wrote four invariant blocks for a new event class, then mutation-proved them three ways as the canon requires.
**Tried / did:** ran the mutations one at a time and read not just WHICH blocks went red but WHAT THEY SAID.
**Result:** ❌ two different mutations — «the class is computed wrong» and «the id list stopped matching» — reddened the same block with the SAME message, and that message was FALSE for the first one: it said the list had stopped catching events when the list had caught the event and merely filed it wrong. ✅ reordered the checks so MATCHING is asked before CLASS (the `means` field is present iff a rule matched at all); the two now produce different, accurate red lines.
**Lesson:** **a mutation run proves the block goes red; it does not prove the block is RIGHT about why — and only reading the red LINE catches the difference.** A guard whose diagnosis misnames the cause is worse than one that merely goes red: it goes red at the right moment and then sends the next session down the wrong corridor, with the authority of a passing test suite behind it. The cheap discipline: when two mutations redden one block, read both messages; if they are identical, the block is asserting a conjunction and its check order is the diagnosis.   → link: plans/29 §8 · `event-logger.runClassInvariants` · EXP-0016
**Repro:** apply each mutation separately and diff the red LINES, not just the red block names.
**Trigger:** a mutation proof where two different mutations redden the same block → read both messages before calling it proved.
**Not for:** blocks that genuinely assert one atomic property — there one message is the whole truth.

### EXP-0126 · 2026-08-23 · ❌→✅ · #guards #suites #false-green #bugs27 #battery
**Context:** needed to add a guard for a new class to the `events` fixture suite.
**Tried / did:** before writing it, checked which battery entry would run it. There is none — the suite has existed since phase 1 and `selftest:all` never called it; it lived only inside `npm run phase1:accept`, a command nobody runs routinely.
**Result:** ✅ the guard was written AND the suite was brought into the battery in the same change. **Then the full inventory was taken — every module with a `--selftest` mode against the battery's list — and the gap was not one suite but SIX: 122 green blocks nobody had ever run.** The battery went 19/1046 → 25/1168; exactly one suite is now outside, and it is the one that WRITES to the card.
**Lesson:** **before adding a guard, ask which command actually RUNS the suite you are adding it to — and when the answer is «none», do not fix the instance, take the inventory.** This project had already paid for the general form (`bugs/27` lived five days inside a suite the battery did not call) and had written the rule into the battery's own comment — yet the rake came back on SIX older suites that predate the rule, and would have come back a seventh time if the find had been treated as one file. A guard's value is (does it go red) × (does anyone run it); a session that verifies only the first half has verified half a guard, and a session that fixes only the file in front of it has closed one instance of a class.
**Second half, and it is the part that nearly went wrong: FIVE of the six were listed in `STATUS.md` as deliberately excluded, with a stated reason** («their run touches something outside the process»). Overriding that silently would have been the real defect. Read the reason and ANSWER it in place: here it turned out false for two of them and true-by-the-letter for three, whose sandboxes are proven by their own output — and by the letter of that reason four suites already IN the battery would have to leave. **A stale exclusion is retired by refuting its reason on the record, never by quietly not seeing it.**
**Repro:** `grep -n "<suite id>" tools/selftest-all.mjs` before writing the block; no hit means the suite is not in the battery.
**Trigger:** adding any check to an existing suite → confirm the suite is in the battery first.
**Not for:** suites deliberately excluded with a written reason — read the reason before overriding it.

### EXP-0125 · 2026-08-23 · ✅ · #owner-machine #observation #two-witnesses #no-sensor
**Context:** the owner reported three things after a reboot («no terminal windows», «no explorer error», «login screen was ENG»), each on a surface the agent has no sensor for.
**Tried / did:** did not stop at his word and did not substitute my own instruments for it. For each claim, found the half a MACHINE can see and measured it: the registry for the layout, `EnumWindows` for the desktop, the event log for the crash record — then stated which half each witness covered.
**Result:** ✅ the instrument found MORE than the eye on one of them (13 console windows exist, 0 visible — including the neighbouring project's two, which the eye could not have counted), and found NOTHING on another (the explorer popup leaves no WER record at all, so only the eye can ever witness it — and one clean restart was recorded as one, not as a fix).
**Lesson:** **when the owner reports an observation the agent cannot make, the answer is not «I believe you» and not «let me re-measure» — it is to find the half a machine CAN see and say explicitly which half each witness covers.** Two witnesses blind to each other's half close a defect properly; one witness stretched over both halves is a claim. The corollary that keeps it honest: where NO instrument exists (the explorer popup), the count of the owner's observations is the evidence, and it is written down as a count so a later session cannot read one success as a proof.   → link: bugs/39 · bugs/38 · AGENT_GUIDE.md → the owner's-machine rule
**Repro:** for any owner-reported symptom, ask «what does this leave behind that I can read?» — a registry value, a window handle, a log record — and measure that, naming what it does NOT cover.
**Trigger:** the owner reports a symptom on his screen or his desktop.
**Not for:** the taste class — there the eye is not half the evidence, it is all of it.

### EXP-0124 · 2026-08-23 · ❌→✅ · #signals #detectors #specificity #sensitivity #n-of-1
**Context:** found that the GPU driver's own provider (`nvlddmkm`) logged two errors INSIDE the rung that killed the machine, seconds before the sampler pulse noticed anything. It looked like a finished detector.
**Tried / did:** instead of proposing it, scored it against **every** machine death in the log — 12 of them — by measuring the gap to the last driver error before each.
**Result:** ❌ the story collapsed and ✅ the finding survived in a smaller shape. Three deaths had an error within 10 minutes (92 s · 9.8 min · 130 s), but **the canonical BSOD of fact 39 had none for two days**, and lone errors on quiet days would have produced false stops.
**Lesson:** a candidate signal is judged on the NEGATIVE cases, not the positive one that made you notice it. The positive case measures **specificity**; only the full set of failures measures **sensitivity**, and a detector missing half the failures is an input to a decision, never the decision. The tell that you are about to skip this: you can name the case that impressed you and cannot name how many cases you checked.   → link: researches/15 · ideas/10 §5.1
**Repro:** `Get-WinEvent` the candidate provider and the death marker into ONE timeline, then print the gap from each death to the previous candidate event — the column of gaps is the verdict.
**Trigger:** any «this signal predicts X» finding, before it reaches a plan or a threshold.
**Not for:** signals that are DEFINITIONS rather than predictors (a bugcheck record does not predict a crash, it reports one).

### EXP-0123 · 2026-08-23 · ✅ · #fix #reuse #twin-call-site #KISS
**Context:** `\KAGO\boot-apply` flashed a console window at every logon; started designing a fix (session-0 task, S4U principal, ownership of the journal file…).
**Tried / did:** before writing any of it, grepped the repo for the same defect at a NEIGHBOURING call site. `tray-launcher.js` carried the cure, its reason, its source (`researches/07` §3) and a `[TESTED]` marker.
**Result:** ✅ the fix became a 20-line twin of an already-proven file, with one deliberate difference (wait + propagate the exit code, because this call site's result is a receipt). The session-0 design would have traded a solved problem for a file-ownership problem.
**Lesson:** when a defect has a sibling call site, the project has probably already paid for the cure — **grep for the cure before designing one.** The comment on the proven file usually names the trap you were about to walk into. This is Occam applied to fixes: a twin of a proven thing beats a new mechanism, and it also keeps the class closed instead of half-closed.   → link: bugs/39 · automation-engine/tray-launcher.js
**Repro:** before designing a fix, `grep -rn "<the symptom's mechanism>" --include=*.js --include=*.mjs .` and read the comments of whatever comes back.
**Trigger:** any defect where a similar component in the same project already does the same kind of thing.
**Not for:** the first occurrence of a class — there is no twin to find yet.

### EXP-0122 · 2026-08-23 · ❌ · #powershell #encoding #windows #ascii-only #EXP-0067
**Context:** wrote diagnostic `.ps1` probes with Russian text in `Write-Output` and in regex literals, using the file tool.
**Tried / did:** ran them with the PowerShell tool. Twice.
**Result:** ❌ both times a PARSER error, not a rendering one — «The string is missing the terminator», «Unexpected token» — with the source echoed back as mojibake. The file tool writes UTF-8 **without BOM**; PowerShell 5.1 reads a BOM-less script as windows-1251, so every Cyrillic character becomes two and the tokenizer breaks on quotes and parentheses.
**Lesson:** the dossier already carried the OUTPUT half of this rake (`Set-Content` writes cp1251); this is the **SOURCE** half and it is worse, because the failure is a parse error in a file that looks perfect in the editor. **Keep throwaway `.ps1` sources ASCII-only.** Cyrillic in a CHILD COMMAND's output is fine — that is runtime, not source. A `.ps1` that must carry Cyrillic needs a UTF-8 BOM, which the file tool does not write.   → link: AGENT_GUIDE.md (environment dossier) · EXP-0067
**Repro:** write `Write-Output 'привет'` to a `.ps1` with the file tool, run it with `powershell.exe -File` — parser error.
**Trigger:** writing ANY `.ps1` this session is about to run → strip non-ASCII from the source first.
**Not for:** `.mjs`/`.json`/`.md` — Node and the file tools read UTF-8 correctly; this is a PowerShell 5.1 property.

### EXP-0121 · 2026-08-23 · ❌ · #selftest #sandbox #production-data #EXP-0025
**Context:** wrote four new blocks for the boot-loop breaker, passing the sandbox journal path as `sb.journal`.
**Tried / did:** ran the suite. Two blocks failed with «строк намерения в журнале 6 вместо 1».
**Result:** ❌ the sandbox helper names that field `sb.jr`, not `sb.journal`. `sb.journal` was `undefined`, so every write fell back to the function's DEFAULT path — the owner's **production** boot journal. Nine fabricated intent lines landed in it. Worse than mess: an orphaned intent for `optimised` is exactly what the new breaker reads as «the machine died with this mode», so my own test spill would have blocked the owner's reboot test. Removed the 12 spilled lines, kept a `correction` record in the journal naming what was taken out and why, and backed up the original.
**Lesson:** **a path parameter with a default is a loaded gun pointed at production, and a typo in the sandbox field name pulls the trigger silently.** EXP-0025 taught this for the vmin store; the same shape came back through a different door. Two cheap defences, both cheaper than the cleanup: (1) in a selftest, build the sandbox path object so a wrong key throws instead of resolving to `undefined` — or read it through a helper that validates; (2) when a block asserts «N lines in the journal», a count that is a MULTIPLE of what you expect is the tell that you are appending to a shared file across runs, not a wrong expectation. I read «6 вместо 1» as a bad assertion for a moment before reading it as a wrong path.   → link: `runs/shell/boot-apply.jsonl` correction line 2026-08-23T14:31 · EXP-0025
**Repro:** pass `undefined` as a path to any `appendX(record, filePath = PRODUCTION_DEFAULT)` and watch it write to production.
**Trigger:** writing a selftest that appends to a journal → assert the sandbox path is a non-empty string BEFORE the first write.

### EXP-0120 · 2026-08-23 · ❌→✅ · #owner-word #guards #invented-flag #whose-permission
**Context:** the owner hit the applier's qualification gate on his own machine and asked, angrily and correctly: «почему какой-то нами же выдуманный флаг нам же мешает?»
**Tried / did:** instead of defending it, asked what REAL value the flag gives. Enumerated honestly: it is a hand-set boolean, measures nothing, and duplicates what the curve document already records per row (`origin:measured` / `inherited` / `lever-limited`). Exactly ONE thing it genuinely protected: the UNATTENDED logon path, where a bad mode would give a boot loop on his working machine.
**Result:** ❌→✅ the flag asked the wrong question. It was invented as a brake on the AGENT (don't apply unproven numbers on your own initiative) but written as a ban on the PROFILE — so it blocked the OWNER, who is the acceptance authority. Rewrote the gate to ask WHO is applying (a named consent string, not a boolean), and moved the one real protection to a mechanism that OBSERVES the actual failure: a write-ahead intent before the boot restore, an orphaned intent meaning «the machine died with this mode on the card».
**Lesson:** **when a guard blocks the owner, the guard is asking the wrong question — a permission check must be about WHO acts and whether a human is present, not about whether an artifact is blessed.** And the general form: **a safety flag that stores a PROMISE («these numbers are fine») is weaker than a mechanism that observes the OUTCOME («the last time we did this, the machine died»)** — the promise is hand-set, drifts, and duplicates knowledge that lives better elsewhere; the observation cannot lie. Third: keep the honest data honest — the fix did NOT flip `qualified` to true, because acceptance genuinely had not happened; what changed was whose permission the gate asks for.   → link: `plans/28` · `remembered-state.bootLoopBreaker` · R15
**Repro:** `--apply <draft>` refuses without a named consent; boot restore refuses when the journal holds an orphaned intent for that profile.
**Trigger:** an owner is blocked by our own invention → ask what real failure it prevents, keep only that, and move it to where it can be observed rather than asserted.

### EXP-0119 · 2026-08-23 · ✅ · #guards #contract-change #red-is-correct
**Context:** changed two contracts in one session (the halt message's meaning; the qualification gate's question). Three existing guard blocks went red on the edits.
**Tried / did:** treated each red as a question — «is the code wrong, or is the block encoding a contract we just deliberately changed?» — and answered it in the block's own comment before rewriting it.
**Result:** ✅ all three were the second kind. Each rewritten block now states WHAT in the contract changed and WHY, and keeps the half that must hold forever (e.g. «without consent a draft is still refused BEFORE the first write»).
**Lesson:** **a guard reddening on your own deliberate change is the guard working, not an obstacle — but rewriting it silently destroys the only record that the contract ever changed.** The discipline that costs nothing and saves the next session: when you edit a block because the contract moved, write the old contract, the new one, and the invariant that survived into the block's comment. A block rewritten without that reads, six months later, as if it had always said this.   → link: `profile-manager` draft blocks · `engine` rollback blocks
**Not for:** a red that is actually a defect — the question must be asked, not assumed.

### EXP-0118 · 2026-08-23 · ❌→✅ · #owner-word #rate-vs-phase #ui #refuted-hypothesis
