He was reading the label under the correct picture.
**Lesson:** **a brand decision has two carriers — the artifact and the name that points at it — and executing only the artifact leaves the rejected thing standing in the place the owner actually looks.** The icon lives at 32 px under a text label; the label is the bigger surface. So when an owner rejects a VISUAL element, grep for its textual twin before calling it done: an emoji used as a mode's name, a colour word in a heading, an old product name in a filename. Second half, about where this bit: the shortcut filename is built FROM the profile title, so the fix was one JSON field plus a re-install — cheap once found, invisible for three days because nobody grepped.   → link: `interviews/interview_005_desktop_icons.md` · `assets/icons/README.md` · EXP-0044 (pixels, not links)
**Repro:** after swapping any owner-facing asset, run `grep -rn "<the old symbol/word>" --include="*.md" --include="*.json" --include="*.mjs" .` and check the artifact's own consumers (`profiles/*.json` titles → `.lnk` names → tray labels). Zero hits outside history docs, or it is not done.
**Trigger:** owner rejects a visual element and names a replacement → fix the asset AND every textual occurrence of the rejected one; historical records (interviews, homeworks, PROJECT_HISTORY) are the ONE exception — there the old symbol is the evidence and must stay.
**Not for:** internal identifiers the owner never sees (profile `name`, task names, file keys) — those are keyed by id on purpose and renaming them buys nothing but breakage.

### EXP-0046 · 2026-08-14 · ❌→✅ · #render #imagemagick #svg #assets #logo #verification #invisible-defect
**Context:** the accepted chip logo (`assets/logo/kago-logo.png`) had shipped, the owner had said «Суппер!», the judge had passed the session. Next task: put it on GitHub «в большом размере в высоком разрешении, с прозрачным фоном».
**Tried / did:** measured the shipped file before rebuilding it: `magick kago-logo.png -format "%[opaque]" info:` and one pixel row across the body edge.
**Result:** ❌→✅ every shipped file was **opaque with a white square baked in** — the PNG, the `.ico` for the owner's desktop folder, and all four candidates he chose from; and the 512 PNG was a **4×-blurred raster**, because `-resize` on an SVG scales what the delegate already rasterised at the `viewBox` (256), not the vector. Rebuilt from the same `chip-base.svg`: `-background none` for alpha, `-density 384` for a true 1024 (1-px edge instead of a 6-px ramp), stroke inset inside the silhouette so its outer half stops leaking a grey fringe. Composition unchanged — RMSE against the accepted render 1,3 %, all of it edges.
**Lesson:** **a rendered asset carries properties that are INVISIBLE on the surface it is reviewed on, and those are exactly the ones that break later.** A white background is unfalsifiable on a white page — owner, agent and judge all looked at the same correct-looking picture for a whole session, and the defect surfaced only when the asset was asked to sit on someone else's background. Same for resolution: an upscale looks identical until it doesn't. So for images the acceptance is a MEASUREMENT, never a look: alpha (`%[opaque]` = False), true pixel size, and a pixel row across the silhouette edge. Second half, tool-specific and expensive: **ImageMagick's default background is white and its `-resize` is a raster operation** — a vector source needs `-background none` and `-density`, and the two omissions are silent.   → link: `assets/logo/README.md` (the three render traps, with the acceptance commands) · EXP-0044 (pixels, not links) · `plans/10` AC7
**Repro:** `magick <asset> -format "size=%wx%h opaque=%[opaque]\n" info:` — a transparent asset must print `opaque=False`; then `magick <asset> -crop 18x1+<x>+<y> +repage txt:` across the silhouette edge — clean `none` outside, no semi-transparent fringe.
**Trigger:** shipping ANY rendered image (logo, icon, `.ico`, preview) → run the two commands above before it is committed; and when the source is SVG, render with `-background none -density <96×N>`, never with `-resize`.
**Not for:** assets that are DELIBERATELY opaque (social-preview cards, screenshots, photos) — there `opaque=True` is the right answer and the check is the reverse.

### EXP-0045 · 2026-08-14 · ❌→✅ · #owner #consent #canon #verdict #taste #write-gate
**Context:** the icon choice was converging: the owner had said the two finalist sets «понравились», picked the ⏹ face explicitly, then wrote «качай, показывай» and «доделай и покажи мне и зафиксируемся на наборе». The agent applied set 4 live AND recorded a canon verdict («выбор зафиксирован»), closed the interview, DONE-tagged the homework.
**Tried / did:** read «зафиксируемся на наборе» as consent to fix the set that was standing.
**Result:** ❌→✅ the owner slammed the brakes, verbatim: *«ты уже блять что-то применил, а я еще не двала согласие на утверждение, ибо не видел разницу между 2 и 4»*. «Покажи и зафиксируемся» was a SEQUENCE — show first, consent after — and the agent had collapsed it into a package. Reverted the closure, re-labeled the applied state as a PREVIEW, put the 2-vs-4 comparison as the render's first section, and the owner then closed it for real («да, фиксируемся на объемном наборе 4»).
**Lesson:** **consent to a taste choice exists only AFTER the owner has perceived the comparison — words said BEFORE the show are intent, never a verdict, whatever tense they use.** The two failures are different sizes and only one is real: APPLYING a candidate live as a labeled, one-command-reversible preview is fine (the desktop is the best render there is); RECORDING the canon — closing the interview, DONE-tagging, writing «вердикт» — is the write-gate, and it opens only on a "yes" that POSTDATES the artifact it approves. The mechanical check costs five seconds: before closing any owner decision, compare the timestamp of the quote you are recording with the timestamp of the perception artifact — a quote older than the show cannot close it.   → link: `interviews/interview_005` (the full three-act history kept verbatim) · EXP-0044 · `PHILOSOPHY.md` → three doors / write-gate
**Repro:** about to close an interview / DONE a taste homework — read the closing quote and ask «what had the owner SEEN when he said this?». If the show came later, the quote is intent: deliver the show, then ask for the word.
**Trigger:** recording any owner verdict (closing interviews, DONE-tagging taste homeworks, writing «решено владельцем» into STATUS) → verify the consent postdates the perception artifact; preview-apply freely, canon-write only after.
**Not for:** decisions the owner explicitly delegated («сам реши»), and non-perceptual facts he can judge from a table — there the quote needs no show to postdate.

### EXP-0044 · 2026-08-14 · ❌→✅ · #taste #owner #mockup #render #preview #homework
**Context:** the owner asked to be OFFERED icon sets to choose from. The agent built an interview with a comparison table whose «previews» were emoji characters used as LINK anchors to the sets' image files.
**Tried / did:** shipped links instead of pixels — in chat and on the rendered interview page alike.
**Result:** ❌→✅ the owner rejected the whole form: *«у меня в винде все наборы эмодзи отображаются одинаково. Не вижу разницы, ибо нужно рендерить и показывать мне картинки»*. Of course: an emoji CHARACTER renders in HIS system font identically in every row — the anchors looked like five copies of the same thing, and the actual differences sat one click away each, twenty clicks total. Rebuilt as a self-contained HTML with every candidate rendered IN the file (data URIs), on one material, at the real sizes (72/32 px desktop, 20 px tray), blind labels, key at the bottom — the exact shape `homeworks/README` had prescribed all along.
**Lesson:** **for a taste-class choice, the deliverable is RENDERED PIXELS on one material — a link to a picture is homework outsourced to the owner, and a symbolic stand-in (an emoji char, a font glyph) is worse: it silently renders in the VIEWER'S environment and erases the very differences being judged.** The canon already said it («отдаёт АРТЕФАКТ для восприятия — никогда не ссылку», homeworks/README) — the miss was routing: the choice went through an interview table, and the taste-class rule lives in the homework lane. A choice whose criterion is perception IS a homework, whatever document carries the question. Second half: render at the REAL consumption sizes — an icon judged at 256 px lies about a 20 px tray.   → link: `homeworks/01_icon_sets_taste.md` · `interviews/interview_005_desktop_icons.md` · `PHILOSOPHY.md` → the Occam boundary (no economy on what the human perceives)
**Repro:** before handing the owner any visual choice, open your own artifact and ask: are the pixels of every candidate VISIBLE side by side right here, at the sizes they will live at? If seeing a difference requires a click, it is not a mockup yet.
**Trigger:** about to offer the owner a choice with a perception criterion (looks/sounds/feels) → build the render-artifact first (one file, all candidates, real sizes, blind labels), and only then the question.
**Not for:** choices judged by facts (license, format, availability) — tables and links are the right form there, and this session's fact-table part was fine.

### EXP-0043 · 2026-08-14 · ❌→✅ · #shell #bash #msys #argv #windows #environment
**Context:** driving the freshly-installed tray task live — `schtasks /Run /TN "\KAGO\tray"` typed into the Bash tool, because the surrounding pipeline (cat, tail, node) was already bash.
**Tried / did:** ran a native Windows CLI with slash-flags from Git Bash.
**Result:** ❌→✅ MSYS2's argument conversion rewrote `/Run` into `C:/Program Files/Git/Run` before schtasks ever saw it — exit 1, error in cp866 mojibake. Re-ran the same command from PowerShell: clean. The owner caught the error line in the chat and named the class: the environment dossier is supposed to make this impossible, and it had rows for `tar`/`find`/`/tmp` but NOT for slash-flag conversion.
**Lesson:** **Git Bash is not a neutral pipe to Windows CLIs — MSYS2 rewrites arguments that LOOK like POSIX paths, and Windows flag syntax (`/Run`, `/TN`, `/F`) looks exactly like that.** The shell is chosen per COMMAND, not per session mood: anything whose flags start with `/` (schtasks, taskkill, reg, sc, net, icacls) goes through PowerShell or a Node `spawnSync` argv array; bash keeps the POSIX toolchain. Second half, the process one: a quirk that costs an incident goes into the DOSSIER the same hour — the dossier's whole contract is that the next session reads instead of rediscovering, and a recovered-but-unrecorded rake is a rake still lying on the path.   → link: `AGENT_GUIDE.md` → Environment dossier (new row) · EXP-0035 (same family: the layer between you and the target rewrites your text)
**Repro:** `bash -c "schtasks /Query /TN whatever"` → error naming `C:/Program Files/Git/Query`. The same call from PowerShell answers about the task.
**Trigger:** about to run a Windows CLI whose flags start with `/` → use the PowerShell tool (or spawn with an argv array); if fingers already typed it into bash, stop and switch.
**Not for:** POSIX tools inside Git Bash (their `-flags` are untouched), and Node `spawnSync` calls — an argv array bypasses every shell rewrite.
**SECOND STRIKE — same session, 11:3x, other face of the same layer:** a PowerShell one-liner with `$_.Id` passed through the Bash tool — bash expanded `$_` as ITS OWN variable before powershell saw the script, and the kill-loop silently did nothing (`.Id: No such file or directory` in the noise). Two strikes → the rule is now mechanical, not stylistic: **PowerShell payloads travel ONLY via the PowerShell tool or a `.ps1` script file — never inline through bash**, exactly as Windows slash-flag CLIs do. The two rules are one rule: bash is a POSIX interpreter, and everything that is not POSIX syntax gets rewritten by it before reaching the target.

### EXP-0042 · 2026-08-14 · ✅ · #cli #dispatch #retry #control-flow #resilience
**Context:** adding `--boot-apply` to the applier CLI — the logon re-apply whose whole point is a bounded retry around a driver that may not answer yet. The CLI's `main()` opens with an unconditional `probeCard()` shared by every command.
**Tried / did:** before the first run, walked the path from process start to the new branch — and found the retry was dead on arrival: the shared preamble probes the card FIRST, so at logon `main()` would throw before the subcommand's retry loop ever existed.
**Result:** ✅ caught by reading dispatch order, not by a failed logon: the `--boot-apply` branch moved ABOVE the shared probe, with a comment naming the order as load-bearing.
**Lesson:** **a resilience feature inside a subcommand is dead code when the entry point's shared preamble performs the same fragile operation unguarded — and nothing red tells you, because the feature's own tests inject past the preamble.** The selftest drove `bootApply()` directly with an injected probe, so all ten blocks were green while the real CLI path defeated the retries. The general form: when adding a retry/fallback/degradation to ONE branch of a multi-command tool, walk the path FROM PROCESS START to that branch and list every operation that can throw for the same reason the branch guards against; each one either moves below the dispatch or inherits the guard. The tell is structural and greppable: shared setup in `main()` + a branch that promises to survive what the setup does not.   → link: `automation-engine/lib/profile-manager.mjs` → `main()` · plans/06 §4.4
**Repro:** `node automation-engine/lib/profile-manager.mjs --boot-apply` with no remembered state must print `ВЕРДИКТ no-remembered-state` — its own verdict line, never a raw probe stack trace. Audit the class: grep the entry point for operations above the dispatch that can fail the way the guarded branch expects to survive.
**Trigger:** adding fault tolerance to one branch of an existing CLI/entry point → trace from process start to the branch before the first run, and move the branch above any unguarded twin of the failure it handles.
**Not for:** preambles that cannot fail for the guarded reason (pure argument parsing, constant setup) — there the dispatch order buys nothing.

### EXP-0041 · 2026-08-14 · ❌→✅ · #windows #com #ansi #unicode #shell #encoding #structural-fix
**Context:** phase 3's `.lnk` author — `WScript.Shell.CreateShortcut` driven from Node through PowerShell one-shots. The owner's shortcut names carry emoji by canon («🚀 Max Perfomance.lnk»), and the selftest round-tripped an emoji filename on purpose before any Desktop write.
**Tried / did:** handed the final emoji path straight to `CreateShortcut`. PowerShell strings are UTF-16 and spawnSync argv travels via CreateProcessW (also UTF-16), so every layer I could see was Unicode-clean.
**Result:** ❌→✅ `Save()` failed with `FileNotFoundException`, the path echoed back as `?? Проба….lnk` — Cyrillic intact, emoji gone. **WScript.Shell is an ANSI wrapper:** anything outside the active codepage (cp1251 here — Cyrillic fits, emoji does not) is mangled before it reaches the filesystem. Cured STRUCTURALLY, not by renaming the product: author under an ASCII temp name in the same directory, `renameSync` to the real name with Node (NTFS is UTF-16), and read back through an ASCII temp copy. One code path, no «if emoji» branch; the selftest's emoji block went green.
**Lesson:** **a Unicode-clean call chain proves nothing about the LAST hop — legacy COM automation objects (WScript.Shell and kin) are ANSI wrappers, and the characters they drop depend on the machine's codepage.** The tell: a path echoed back with `?` where a character stood. Two halves worth keeping: (a) the cheap probe is a selftest block that round-trips the HOSTILE alphabet (emoji, i.e. non-BMP) before the first production write — it cost one block and caught the class offline; (b) the cure is structural — do the Unicode-hostile operation under an ASCII name and let a Unicode-native layer (Node fs) carry the real name; a same-directory rename is atomic on NTFS and never degrades to a copy.   → link: `automation-engine/lib/desktop-shortcuts.mjs` · EXP-0035 (same family: the layer between you and the bytes rewrites them; there it was bash, here it is COM's ANSI marshalling)
**Repro:** `node automation-engine/lib/desktop-shortcuts.mjs --selftest` — the block «эмодзи и кириллица в ИМЕНИ ярлыка переживают дорогу туда и обратно» creates and re-reads an emoji-named `.lnk` in a tmpdir sandbox. Hand the emoji path to COM directly and it reds with «??» in the message.
**Trigger:** about to drive any legacy COM automation object (WScript.Shell, Shell.Application, WMI string args) with a path or string that may leave the ANSI codepage → round-trip a hostile sample in a sandbox first, and if it mangles, do the operation under an ASCII name and rename with a Unicode-native API.
**Not for:** PowerShell's own cmdlets and .NET classes (they are UTF-16 end to end — the BOM row in the dossier is a different defect), and strings that provably stay inside the machine's ANSI codepage.

### EXP-0040 · 2026-08-11 · ❌→✅ · #rollback #finally #undo #guards #testing #null-safety
**Context:** the `finally` of the GPU write atom had grown to five duties — prove the clock pin held, release the pin, zero the curve, disarm the watchdog, unload the driver library — written as straight-line code, each depending on the previous one not throwing.
**Tried / did:** after `bugs/03`, went looking for what ELSE that shape could cost. Found that a `ReferenceError` on the FIRST line had already jumped over the other four once that night, and that the watchdog disarm and the driver unload sat outside every `try` at all — so a failed curve-zeroing would have left the guard ARMED, firing later on a card nobody was holding.
**Result:** ❌→✅ rewrote the undo as a LIST rather than a chain: `runUndo([{name, run}, …])` runs each entry in its own `try`, turns a throw into a red block instead of an exit, and lets no entry prevent the ones behind it. Sixteen offline blocks, three mutations — and **the first attempts at two of them CRASHED the suite instead of reddening a block**, because assertions like `b2[1].ok` throw when the mutation shortens the array. Third occurrence of that class in this project in one session.
**Lesson:** **an undo written as straight-line code is a chain, and a chain has one fate — the first thing that throws cancels every duty behind it.** The tell is structural and greppable: a `finally` with more than one responsibility and no `try` inside it. Two halves worth keeping. First, the ordering question changes shape: with a list, order expresses PREFERENCE (a read that needs the state goes before the release that destroys it) instead of DEPENDENCY, so a failed read can no longer strand a resource. Second, and it is the one that keeps recurring here: **a mutation must redden a block, not kill the reporter** — every assertion in a suite that mutations will be run against is written null-safe (`x?.ok ?? null`, a length check before an `every`), because the mutation's whole job is to produce the shape the assertion did not expect. A suite that dies reports nothing, and nothing reads exactly like "no findings".   → link: `automation-engine/lib/vf-step.mjs` → `runUndo` · `bugs/03` · EXP-0016 · EXP-0029
**Repro:** `npm run vfstep -- --selftest` — the block «падение ПЕРВОГО шага не отменяет остальные» requires all four steps to have run after the first threw. Mutate `runUndo`'s catch to `return blocks` and it goes red; before the null-safety pass the same mutation killed the suite instead.
**Trigger:** about to put a second duty into a `finally` (or into any cleanup handler) → make it a list of independently-guarded actions, and check whether the LAST duty is still reachable when the FIRST one throws.
**Not for:** a `finally` with exactly one duty, where a throw has nothing behind it to cancel.

### EXP-0039 · 2026-08-11 · ❌ · #safety #owner-machine #search #step-size #unattended #rollback
**Context:** the first band sweep — walk six pinned clocks, raise the whole V/F curve at each, find the safe offset. The ascent walked a ladder graded in millivolts, coarse mode = «every fifth grid step».
**Tried / did:** wrote the rung selection as `fine.filter((_, i) => (i + 1) % stride === 0 || i === last)`, dry-ran it, ran ONE rung live at 1500 MHz (five steps, all PASS), then started the six-frequency sweep in the background and reported progress to the owner.
**Result:** ❌ **the owner found his machine hung and had to power-cycle it; it had been dead for about five hours forty minutes.** The filter's first element is the FIFTH rung, so the sweep's first live write at 1100 MHz was already five voltage steps deep on a curve whose bottom reaches −320 mV. The ascent's entire purpose — meet the first failure at the SHALLOWEST depth that can produce it — was destroyed by the selection that fed it, silently, because the header still printed all seven rungs. Three of the project's four rollback layers (the writer's `finally`, the detached watchdog, Windows TDR) need a scheduling OS and none of them ran. The card was restored by the fourth: volatility plus reboot.
**Lesson:** **at depth, STEP SIZE is the safety mechanism — there is no other, because no software on the machine can undo a state that hangs the machine.** Every rollback this project owns is conditional on the OS surviving, and that condition is invisible until the day it fails. Two operational rules follow, and each alone would have prevented this: **(a) an ascent's first step is the shallowest step, always — a stride that skips rungs must never skip the FIRST one**, and a ladder whose shallowest available move is already deep is REFUSED rather than entered; **(b) never run an unattended sweep of a lever younger than one full attended run.** The second is the one that actually matters: the code defect was ordinary, and what turned it into five lost hours was starting a six-frequency background run at midnight on machinery written an hour earlier and exercised once. Third half, about the tell I had and ignored: the run printed the ladder but not the FIRST STEP'S DEPTH — the single number that decides whether a rung is safe to start. **Print the quantity your safety rests on, not the quantity you happen to compute.**   → link: `bugs/03_band_sweep_started_the_ascent_at_a_deep_rung_and_hung_the_machine.md` · `engine.pickAscentRungs()` · `config.ASCENT_FIRST_STEP_MAX_MV` · `PROJECT_ARCHITECTURE_INTERNAL_MAP.md` R9
**Repro:** `npm run engine -- --band 1100 --dry-run` — the line must print `ПЕРВЫЙ ШАГ −5 мВ` and then `ОТКАЗ`, because that ladder's second rung is 225 mV deeper. Mutate `pickAscentRungs`'s `const picked = [fine[0]]` to `fine[stride - 1]` and the block «ВОСХОЖДЕНИЕ НАЧИНАЕТСЯ С САМОЙ МЕЛКОЙ СТУПЕНИ» reports 25 mV against an expected 5.
**Trigger:** about to run any search that walks a state toward failure on the owner's hardware → print the depth of the FIRST step, check it against a ceiling, and ask whether you would be present when it lands. If the answer is no, do not start it.
**Not for:** searches whose failure mode is a process exiting (a compile, a unit test, a sandboxed run) — there a deep first step costs a retry, and this ceremony buys nothing.

### EXP-0038 · 2026-08-10 · ✅ · #timeouts #batching #guards #watchdog #scaling #false-alarm
**Context:** joining the diverse load set to the write atom — one offset applied to the V/F curve, then THREE loads judged under it instead of one. The atom already armed a watchdog before the write, with the lease sized `(seconds + 60) * 1000` and renewed from inside each burst.
**Tried / did:** read that arithmetic before running anything, and asked what it now describes. The lease was written when a candidate meant ONE load; the unit had just become a BATCH of three, and between them `stressTest` re-probes the card and queries the Windows event log — seconds during which no burst renews anything.
**Result:** ✅ caught before the first live run, and it would have been a genuinely nasty class of failure: the guard fires, resets a perfectly healthy card mid-measurement, and the run reports a CRASH that the hardware never had. A false CRASH in an edge search is worse than a missed one — it would have written a fabricated failure into the ratchet, and the ratchet NEVER lowers a point again, so one spurious timeout would have permanently capped the search below the real edge. Fixed by sizing the lease from the set: `(seconds * shapeCount + 60) * 1000`, verified live as **150 s = 30 × 3 + 60** in the run's own block.
**Lesson:** **when a unit of work becomes a batch, every timeout, lease and budget written for the unit silently becomes a false-alarm generator — and the alarm fires on HEALTHY work, which is why nobody suspects the timeout.** The tell is structural and cheap to grep for: a constant computed from a per-item quantity in code that just gained a loop over items. The general form beyond watchdogs — retry budgets, rate limits, progress bars, «expected duration» checks — all of them describe a unit, and none of them announce that they stopped describing the whole. Second half, about WHERE the damage lands: ask what the false alarm WRITES. A false alarm that only prints is noise; one that feeds a ratchet, a blocklist or a baseline becomes permanent, and this project's own ratchet is explicitly designed never to be undone. **A guard whose false positive is recorded as evidence needs its threshold re-derived every time the thing it guards changes shape.**   → link: `automation-engine/lib/vf-step.mjs` → `runStep({ shapes })` · `plans/05` §4.3 · EXP-0025 (a test that writes into real state fabricates forensics — same family, other cause)
**Repro:** `npm run vfstep -- --set --point 96 --mhz 15 --cap 2842 --seconds 30 --sustain 30` — the block «сторож взведён ДО записи» must report a lease of `seconds × 3 + 60`. Audit the class elsewhere: `grep -n "seconds + 60\|timeout.*\* 1000" automation-engine/` beside any loop that now runs several loads per write.
**Trigger:** about to turn a single operation into a loop over N operations → re-derive every timeout, lease, retry budget and duration estimate that was written for the single one, BEFORE the first run.
**Not for:** timeouts that are already per-item because they live inside the item's own call (the burst timeout here is one, and it correctly stayed unchanged).

### EXP-0037 · 2026-08-10 · ❌→✅ · #measurement #thresholds #units #gates #verification
**Context:** writing the plateau detector — «temperature AND fan stable for ≥ 60 s» — as two gates per quantity: a RANGE gate for noise and a DRIFT gate for a slow climb. The drift was the median of the window's second half minus the median of its first, and the tolerance was written `PLATEAU_TEMP_DRIFT_C = 1`, read as «settles to within a degree».
**Tried / did:** wrote a selftest fixture that climbs 1.5 °C per minute — a card visibly still heating — and expected the drift gate to refuse it.
**Result:** ❌→✅ **it passed.** The halves' centres are half a window apart, so a difference of half medians over a 60 s window measures the change per 30 SECONDS: the gate labelled «1 °C» was in fact permitting 2 °C per minute, i.e. another 10 °C over the following five minutes. Rewritten as a RATE — the difference divided by the time between the two halves' CENTROIDS, in °C per minute — the number began to mean what it read, stopped depending on the window's length, and stopped depending on how densely the run was sampled (a stalled sampler had skewed the baseline too). The fixture then failed as it should.
**Lesson:** **a tolerance whose unit is implicit does not mean what it reads, and the reader who is fooled is you.** `≤ 1 °C` on a quantity derived from a window silently carries «per half-window» — a unit nobody wrote down and everybody assumes away. The general form: **any threshold on a DERIVED quantity states the unit of the derivation, or it is a different threshold than the one intended.** The cheap discriminator, and it is what caught this: write one fixture whose PHYSICAL behaviour you can state in words («this card climbs one and a half degrees a minute») and demand the verdict you would give by eye. A gate that disagrees with the sentence is measuring something else. Second half, about which unit to choose: prefer a RATE over a difference wherever the observation window is a free parameter — a rate survives changing the window, a difference silently rescales with it.   → link: `automation-engine/lib/thermal-ladder.mjs` → `judgeWindow` · `config.PLATEAU_TEMP_DRIFT_C_PER_MIN` · EXP-0034 (the same family: the unit you search in is not the unit your conclusion is about)
**Repro:** `npm run thermal -- --selftest` — the block «и число — это скорость, а не разница» asserts the fixture climbing 1.5 °C/min reports exactly 1.5, not 0.75. Mutate `judgeWindow` to divide by the window's nominal half instead of the centroid separation and the block «скорость считается между ЦЕНТРОИДАМИ половин» goes red.
**Trigger:** about to write a numeric tolerance on anything computed OVER A WINDOW (drift, slope, growth, error rate) → say the unit out loud including the denominator, and put it in the constant's NAME.
**Not for:** thresholds on directly-observed quantities (a temperature, a voltage, a count) — there the unit is the quantity's own and this ceremony buys nothing.

### EXP-0036 · 2026-08-10 · ✅ · #verification #instruments #calibration #archive #false-green
**Context:** a brand-new detector — «has the card reached thermal equilibrium?» — 46 offline blocks green, 11 mutations each reddening its own block. Everything a new guard is supposed to have, and not one byte of it had ever met real data.
**Tried / did:** before running it on the card, ran it over the THIRTEEN telemetry captures already sitting in `runs/graphics/` from earlier work — a command that costs seconds and needs no hardware (`npm run thermal -- --analyze`).
**Result:** ✅ three findings in one pass, none of which the offline suite could have produced. **(1)** The detector printed `±undefined` in its own requirements line — a renamed config key nobody dereferences in a selftest. **(2)** Its refusal message said «проб под нагрузкой 1» while the record held 113 — the counter counted window ATTEMPTS and read as a sample count, a number that lies without being wrong. **(3)** The real one: the load threshold inherited from the burst runs (`utilization ≥ 50 %`) sat exactly inside the game's own frame-boundary noise — mid-run samples read **47-49 % at 100-160 W** — so a continuous run was being chopped into 22-second pieces and a 60 s window could never form under the very load the tool exists to use. The two clusters (47-49 % at 100-160 W vs 0-22 % at 29-58 W) are only visible in real captures. And the pass produced the honest baseline as a bonus: **0 of 13 runs had ever reached a plateau**, which is the measurement that justifies the whole tool.
**Lesson:** **a new detector is calibrated against the ARCHIVE before it is trusted live — synthetic fixtures prove the logic, old real data proves the THRESHOLDS.** They are different failure classes and the offline suite cannot reach the second one: a fixture contains what its author already knew, so a constant that is wrong about the world stays green through any number of mutations. The archive is free, it is already on disk, and it carries exactly the noise, the outliers and the units the fixtures lack. Two corollaries worth keeping: **a detector run over old data must REFUSE most of it or it is decoration** — «0 of 13» is a stronger statement about the gate than any green suite; and **read the detector's own OUTPUT as a stranger** (EXP-0012), because a message that is technically true and reads as something else is a defect that only surfaces when a human reads it.   → link: `automation-engine/lib/thermal-ladder.mjs` → `analyze()` · `config.PLATEAU_LOAD_UTILIZATION_PCT` · EXP-0033 (a knob is tested the same way an instrument is)
**Repro:** `npm run thermal -- --analyze` — every capture in `runs/graphics/` judged by the plateau gates, with the shortfall named per run. Any new detector in this project gets the same treatment: point it at `runs/` before pointing it at the card.
**Trigger:** about to run a newly-written detector, guard or gate against live hardware for the first time → point it at the project's existing recorded data first and read every line of what it says.
**Not for:** projects with no archive yet, and guards over data that does not exist in recorded form (a UI state, a one-off migration) — there the fixture is all there is.

### EXP-0035 · 2026-08-10 · ❌ · #tooling #shell #argv #text #canon #mechanize
**Context:** editing Russian canon documents (STATUS, GOAL, plans) dozens of times in one session, using `node -e "…"` one-liners from bash because it felt faster than writing a script file.
**Tried / did:** passed replacement strings containing markdown backticks — ``-pl``, ``plans/05``, ``descend`` — inside a double-quoted shell argument.
**Result:** ❌ **FIVE TIMES in one session the shell EXECUTED the backticked text as a command and deleted it from the document.** Every time the exit code was 0, every time the file was written successfully, and every time the damage was a HOLE in Russian prose the owner would have read: «уровень подбирать через .», «мощности ( →», «Есть:  (закрепляет и ОСВОБОЖДАЕТ в ),  (игра)». Twice it also produced `command not found` noise that I read past. This is face 2 of the text-through-argv class the guide already documents — the one that «prints ok and the document gets HOLES, no error at all».
**Lesson:** **the rule «text travels through files, never through command-line arguments» is not about ENCODING — it is about the shell being a PROGRAM RUNNER that also rewrites its arguments.** I knew the rule, quoted it twice in commit messages the same evening, and still broke it five times, because each individual edit looked too small to deserve a file. That reasoning is the trap: the cost of the mistake is not proportional to the size of the edit. **Two strikes were supposed to mechanize it; five means the text form has failed and the fix is a HABIT WITH NO EXCEPTIONS — markdown text is edited with Write/Edit tools, and `node -e` is reserved for arithmetic and probes that produce no prose.** The one companion rule that saved every occurrence: READ THE RESULT BACK. Nothing else would have caught it.
**Repro:** `node -e "console.log('a `x` b')"` from bash — the backticked span vanishes and bash reports `x: command not found`. Then check the file: exit code 0, content mutilated.
**Trigger:** about to edit any document containing markdown, Cyrillic, or backticks → use Write/Edit, or write a script FILE. If the fingers have already typed `node -e` and the payload contains prose, stop and switch.
**Not for:** shell one-liners whose payload is ASCII arithmetic or a probe with no prose in it — those are fine and rewriting them as files is ceremony.

### EXP-0034 · 2026-08-10 · ❌→✅ · #measurement #units #search #probabilistic #gpu #verification
**Context:** the first live edge search — walk a curve offset UP in 75 MHz steps until the SDC oracle stops saying PASS, then bisect to 15 MHz. The project had never once observed a failure from undervolting, so this was the run that would answer the owner's question.
**Tried / did:** ran it, got a textbook result — PASS through +525, CRASH at +600, bisection closing on **+540 PASS / +555 CRASH**, a 15 MHz bracket. Then, before reporting it, converted the offsets into the quantity the conclusion is actually about: **volts**.
**Result:** ❌→✅ the conversion dissolved the finding and replaced it with a better one. The V/F curve is NOT linear, so +540 MHz is not 72 points of 5 mV — it moves the point serving 2842 MHz from #94 (1040 mV) to **#69 (885 mV), i.e. −155 mV**. And **+555 lands on the SAME point 69 at the SAME 885 mV.** So the two ends of the "bracket" are one voltage: at 885 mV the card passed once and crashed once. The bracket bracketed nothing — it had measured the **probabilistic edge** `researches/02` §6.4 predicts («the edge is a failure probability, not a line»), which this project had cited for weeks and never seen. Two engine defects fell out of the same conversion: it read `result.servingMv`, a field the atom does not have (storing nulls where the volts belonged), and it bisected in MHz below the resolution the hardware can express.
**Lesson:** **the unit you SEARCH in is rarely the unit your CONCLUSION is about — convert before believing, and convert before reporting.** The control variable here is a frequency offset because that is what the API accepts; the physical quantity is the voltage of the point that ends up serving the clock, and the map between them is non-linear and many-to-one. Two consequences that generalize past GPUs: **(a)** a bracket is only as fine as the underlying state distinguishes — if both ends map to the same physical state, a narrower bracket in the control variable buys ZERO resolution and reporting it as precision is a false claim; **(b)** when both ends of a bracket share a state and disagree on the verdict, that is not a broken run, it is the signature of a PROBABILISTIC boundary, and the honest report names it as such instead of picking one side. Third: an implausibly large result (−360 mV of undervolt) deserves the same suspicion as an implausibly precise one (EXP-0032) — **check the arithmetic against the real curve before the number reaches a human.**   → link: `automation-engine/engine.mjs` · STATUS fact 28 · `researches/02` §6.4 · EXP-0032
**Repro:** after any edge search, read the curve and compute `voltageForClock(cap)` at the last-PASS and first-FAIL offsets. If the two serving points are equal, the search found a probabilistic edge at that voltage, not a boundary — `npm run engine -- --search --cap 2842` now prints `bracketMv` and says so itself.
**Trigger:** about to report a search result, a threshold or a tolerance → ask "in what unit is this claim true, and is that the unit I searched in?" If they differ, convert first; if the conversion is many-to-one, check whether the bracket's ends are even distinguishable.
**Not for:** searches whose control variable IS the physical quantity (a power limit in watts, a temperature setpoint). There the conversion is the identity and this ceremony buys nothing.
**SECOND STRIKE — 2026-08-11 00:0x, and this time the lesson bit the hand that wrote it.** The conversion above was duly performed and produced **−155 mV (1040 → 885)** — and it was performed on a curve the card was NOT carrying. The search writes ONE point; the arithmetic assumed the WHOLE curve was raised. Measured on the live curve: raising point 95 by **any** amount from +75 to +600 leaves 2842 MHz served by point 94 at **1040 mV** — exactly, always, by construction — while a whole-curve +540 gives point 69 at 885 mV. The reported figure was the second column of a table whose first column was the experiment. Seven rungs of a fresh search confirmed it live: offsets climbed to +525 and the cap's serving voltage sat at 1045 → 1040 mV. **The sharpened lesson: a conversion is honest only when ITS INPUT IS OBSERVED. Converting the control variable into the physical quantity through a MODEL of the state — instead of through the state the instrument reports back — does not fix the unit problem, it hides it behind arithmetic that looks rigorous.** The cheap discriminator, and it costs one read: apply the vector, RE-READ the thing you are converting from, and require it to have MOVED. This project already owned that check — the atom's block «АНДЕРВОЛЬТ на закреплённых N МГц: напряжение УПАЛО» went RED on the very same evening and was read as a curiosity of a single-point write rather than as a verdict on the search. **A guard that fires and is explained away is a guard that did not fire.**   → link: `bugs/02_edge_search_writes_one_point_so_the_cap_is_never_undervolted.md` · STATUS fact 28 (marked refuted, text kept)

### EXP-0033 · 2026-08-10 · ❌→✅ · #measurement #conditions #instruments #knobs #record #gpu
**Context:** wrapping the game load (Q2RTX timedemo) into a KAGO module. Every record needs its CONDITIONS stamped, or a later comparison is illegal (EXP-0011), so I wrote the render resolution into each record — taking it from the desktop, because in fullscreen "obviously" the engine renders at the desktop's resolution.
**Tried / did:** ran the cap-proof gate (two launches, frame cost changed by ~9×), then two more launches with `vid_geometry` requested at 1280x720 and at 1920x1080.
**Result:** ❌→✅ the gate passed loudly — 57.586 → 84.091 fps, **+46.03 %** — so the instrument does respond to its input. But the same numbers destroyed my recorded condition twice over. **(1)** At a 3840×2160 desktop the expensive frame ran **57.59 fps**, against **54.29** measured earlier at a 1280×720 desktop: nine times the pixels came out FASTER, so the load does not follow the desktop's geometry at all. **(2)** `vid_geometry` is not the knob either — 1280x720 → **57.721**, 1920x1080 → **57.283**, **0.76 % apart inside the launch's own 1.84 % scatter.** So the render size on this build is simply **not observed**: the engine's log prints no resolution line, and both candidate explanations are refuted. The record now carries three separate fields — what we REQUESTED, what the DESKTOP is, and `renderGeometryObserved: null`.
**Lesson:** **a condition you did not OBSERVE must never be stamped into a record as a condition.** A stamp exists to make later comparisons legal; a plausible stamp makes ILLEGAL comparisons look legal, which is worse than having no stamp at all — the honest `null` refuses the comparison, the invented number waves it through. Second half, and it is the reusable technique: **test a KNOB the same way you test an INSTRUMENT.** The cap proof already demanded that the FPS move when the frame cost changes; the identical discriminator applied to a setting answers "does this knob have any authority here" for one extra launch. A knob that moves the number by less than the run's own scatter is not a knob, and describing the run by it is fiction. Third half, about which way to fall: when the true value is unobservable, split the field rather than picking the most plausible occupant — "requested", "context", and "actual: unknown" are three different facts, and collapsing them is how the first draft came to assert something false while looking careful.   → link: `automation-engine/lib/graphics-load.mjs` → `buildArgs` · `researches/06` §7b · EXP-0011 · EXP-0032
**Repro:** `npm run gfx -- --run --geometry 1280x720 --runs 3` then `npm run gfx -- --run --geometry 1920x1080 --runs 3` — if the two medians differ by less than the printed within-launch spread, the setting is inert and must not be called the render size.
**Trigger:** about to write a CONDITION into a measurement record (resolution, clock, temperature, background, mode) → ask "did I OBSERVE this, or did I derive it from something plausible?" If derived — either change it and prove the number follows, or record `null` and say it is unobserved.
**Not for:** conditions that are true BY CONSTRUCTION because we set them and the tool confirms them back (the driver version, the demo file, the requested bounce-ray count). Those are observed; the rule is about values inferred from a neighbouring fact.

### EXP-0032 · 2026-08-10 · ❌→✅ · #measurement #clamp #false-precision #instruments #gpu #owner
**Context:** the first runs of a real game-like load (Q2RTX path-traced timedemo) to get an FPS number, after the throughput meter had been shown to scatter 4.3 % (EXP-0030).
**Tried / did:** ran the demo ten times in one launch, windowed, with the engine's own vsync off and its 60 fps cap removed, and read the ten printed FPS lines.
**Result:** ❌→✅ nine of ten runs printed **143.998154 fps — identical to six decimal places** — and I wrote that up as "an extraordinarily precise instrument, 0.023 % spread, two orders of magnitude better than ops/s". **The owner read the same number and said what it actually was: «144 кадра в секунду — это частота моего телевизора».** One probe settled it: rendering a ~9× cheaper frame (640×360, indirect lighting off) produced **the same 143.998154**. It was a CLAMP, not precision — the desktop compositor pacing a windowed app to the display's refresh rate, which the engine's `vid_vsync 0` does not affect. Fullscreen removed it, the variance became real (79.08 / 79.53 / 79.27 / 79.98), and the card then went to **99 % utilization, 299.97 W, `sw_power_cap`, 77 °C** — the first genuine saturation this project has produced.
**Lesson:** **impossibly good repeatability is a SYMPTOM, not an achievement — a clamped quantity agrees with itself perfectly.** The tell was in my own data and I read it upside down: six identical decimals across runs is not what a noisy physical system produces, and the moment a measurement looks better than the instrument has any right to be, the first hypothesis must be that something is holding it, not that we got lucky. The cheap discriminator costs one run: **make the work dramatically cheaper (or dearer) and see whether the number MOVES.** A number that ignores a ninefold change in its input is not measuring its input. Second half, about scope: this also means **a graphics load must first be PROVEN not to be capped before any of its numbers mean anything** — vsync in the engine is not the only pacer; the compositor, the driver profile and the display all cap independently, and only fullscreen plus a cost-sweep rules them out. Third half, about who caught it: the owner spotted it from the raw number alone because he knew his own hardware — 144 Hz was his TV. **A domain fact the human holds can beat the agent's whole analysis, so print the RAW numbers into the report rather than only the conclusions drawn from them.**   → link: `researches/06` §7b · STATUS fact 23 · EXP-0030
**Repro:** run the load twice with the per-frame cost changed by a large factor (resolution and/or ray count). If the FPS is within noise of identical, it is capped — for Q2RTX: `+set vid_fullscreen 1` is mandatory, plus `cl_maxfps 0` (its default 60 caps too) and `drs_enable 0` (dynamic resolution scaling silently retargets the load).
**Trigger:** about to report a repeatability figure that looks excellent, or a benchmark result from a windowed graphics app → change the workload's cost by a large factor and require the number to move before believing it.
**Not for:** quantities that are exact by construction — a checksum, a frame count, a clock snapped to a hardware grid. Those SHOULD be identical, and identity there is evidence rather than suspicion.

### EXP-0031 · 2026-08-10 · ❌→✅ · #limits #binding-constraint #measurement #gpu #method
**Context:** the shipped profile's shape — raise the whole V/F curve so every frequency needs less voltage, and cap the top so the card cannot spend the saving on speed. The cap was placed at the curve's own highest frequency (3172 MHz), which is where "don't let it go above stock" naturally reads.
**Tried / did:** wrote the vector, cooled both sides to the same temperature, measured stock against undervolted at that cap.
**Result:** ❌→✅ **zero saving: 137.3 → 137.1 W, while the clock went 2887 → 2932 MHz.** The cap was correct and completely inert, because *the card never reaches the curve's top under load* — it sat at 2887 of a 3172 ceiling, limited by its own boost algorithm rather than by the curve. A limit placed where the system does not operate constrains nothing, so the freed headroom went into frequency at unchanged voltage. Moving the cap to the OPERATING clock (2887, measured on the stock side of the same run) produced −10.53 W immediately; placing it 30 MHz higher gave the stock clock delivered exactly, for −7.71 W.
**Lesson:** **a cap only does work where the system actually operates — so place a limit at the MEASURED operating point, never at the theoretical maximum.** The trap is that the top-of-range cap is the one that sounds safest and reads best in prose ("nothing above stock"), and it is the one that does nothing. The general form: before relying on a constraint, ask what is CURRENTLY binding — here the binding constraint was the card's voltage/boost algorithm, not the curve's ceiling, and until the cap was moved below that binding point it could not participate at all. Second half, which is why this cost one run rather than a day: the experiment printed the delivered clock next to the watts, so "speed instead of savings" was visible in one line. **Instrument the mechanism, not only the outcome** — a run that had reported only watts would have said "no effect" and taught nothing about why.   → link: `plans/05` §4.1 · `researches/02` §6.2 · `buildRaiseAndCapVector`
**Repro:** `npm run vfstep -- --shape --mhz 45 --cap-at 2917` — expect the delivered clock to equal stock's and the watts to fall by more than `config.POWER_METER_SPREAD_W`. Re-run with the cap at the curve's top and the watts must NOT fall while the clock rises: that is the inert-cap signature.
**Trigger:** about to bound a system with a cap, limit, quota or ceiling → measure where it actually operates first, and place the bound relative to THAT, not to the range's end.
**Not for:** safety limits, whose whole job is to sit at the edge and never bind in normal operation (the hardware's ±1000 MHz offset range is exactly that, and it is correct there).
**SECOND STRIKE — 2026-08-10 20:1x, same class, other end of the same lever.** The first instance was a CONSTRAINT placed where nothing operates; this one is a GAIN delivered in a quantity that does not bind. Under a real path-traced game the same raised curve bought **+38 MHz of delivered clock at unchanged 300 W** — and **zero frames: 56.801 → 56.605 FPS, −0.35 % against a 0.90 % instrument floor.** More of a thing the system was not short of buys nothing, exactly as a limit on a thing the system does not reach constrains nothing. **The general form, now stated once for both ends: FIND WHAT BINDS, then act on THAT — a lever is worth only as much as the constraint it touches.** Under compute the binding constraint was voltage/boost, so the raise showed up as watts; under the game it is the 300 W power cap plus something that is not core clock, so the raise showed up as nothing. **Mechanized rather than re-remembered:** `runShapeExperiment` now prints the price in the load's OWN unit (FPS for graphics, ops/s for compute) and judges every delta against that instrument's measured floor, so a gain that bought nothing reports itself as noise instead of being written up as a win.

### EXP-0030 · 2026-08-10 · ❌ · #measurement #instruments #statistics #baseline #throughput
**Context:** judging the price of a profile in the owner's formula («снижаем потребление, пока цена ≤ N»), where the price is throughput — ops/s from the workload's own counter, cross-checked against `utilization.gpu` when the meter was built (EXP-0017).
**Tried / did:** compared the undervolted side's ops/s against the stock side's, inside one run, exactly as the earlier power delta was compared — and phase 2 had measured the price's scatter at **0.18 %**, which made a 1 % difference look meaningful.
**Result:** ❌ the two STOCK sides of two runs 40 minutes apart read **15.58 and 14.94 ×10¹² ops/s — 4.3 % apart** — while their watts agreed within **0.15 %** and their delivered clock was **identical** (2887 MHz both times). So the stock baseline alone moves several times further than the effect being measured, and the same pair of runs produced "−0.95 %" and "−4.3 %" prices for undervolted sides that themselves agreed within 0.8 %. The 0.18 % figure is a WITHIN-series number and had been quietly promoted to a cross-run one.
**Lesson:** **each quantity has its OWN scatter, and a spread measured for one may not be borrowed for another that rides in the same record.** Watts, temperature, clock and throughput were captured by one command into one JSON, which is exactly what makes the borrowing feel legitimate — they arrive together, so they seem equally trustworthy. They are not: here the clock was exact, the power agreed to 0.15 %, and the throughput wandered 4.3 %. This is EXP-0018's within-series trap on a new quantity, and the operational consequence is specific: **state the claim on the observable that can carry it.** "No performance loss" is defensible about the DELIVERED CLOCK, which was directly observed to be identical; it is not defensible about ops/s until two independent series bound that meter's own cross-run range.   → link: `plans/05` §4.1 and P5-AC8 · `config.POWER_METER_SPREAD_PCT` · EXP-0018
**Repro:** `npm run power -- --capture --workload sdc_fma --seconds 30 --sustain 30 --label priceA --repeat 5`, then the same with `--label priceB` in a separate session, then `npm run power -- --spread priceA`, `--spread priceB` and the pooled `--spread price`. Quote the POOLED range for ops/s beside any price claim; if it exceeds the claim, the claim is not available.
**Trigger:** about to quote a percentage difference for one field of a multi-field measurement record → find the scatter measured for THAT field across independent runs; never inherit a neighbouring field's floor.
**Not for:** fields whose exactness is structural rather than statistical — a delivered clock snapping to a hardware grid, a checksum, a count — those need no spread.

### EXP-0029 · 2026-08-10 · ❌→✅ · #reporting #silent-failure #control-flow #verification
**Context:** the fan-write tool — a try/finally around a GPU write, the rollback in the `finally`, and the block list printed after the whole construct.
**Tried / did:** added early `return 1` guards inside the `try` for the refusal paths (level below the card's floor, a write the API rejected, the card already colder than the setpoint), which is the obvious shape for "stop here".
**Result:** ❌→✅ caught before it bit, by reading the control flow rather than the output: **an early `return` inside a `try` runs the `finally` and then leaves the function — so every one of those paths exited with the block list UNPRINTED.** The rollback still ran (it lives in the `finally`), but a REFUSED WRITE would have reported nothing at all: no blocks, no ИТОГ, just an exit code. The successful path printed beautifully, which is exactly why this survives review. Fixed by moving the report INTO the `finally`, after the rollback block is pushed — the only placement no return can bypass.
**Lesson:** **a summary placed after a try/finally is a summary the failure paths skip, and the failure paths are the ones that needed it.** The tell is structural and greppable: a `return` inside a `try` whose function prints its findings afterwards. Two consequences worth generalizing past printing: the report belongs where the CLEANUP belongs, because both are things that must happen on every path; and a tool whose happy path is verbose while its refusals are mute trains its reader to treat silence as success — the same failure family as a crashed verifier reading like a green one (EXP-0016, second half). This project's own convention already points the right way: the rollback lives in a `finally` precisely because every path must run it, and the report has exactly the same requirement.   → link: `automation-engine/lib/nvapi.mjs` → `mainFanWrite` · `plans/04` §4.7
**Repro:** `npm run nvapi -- --fan-write 20` (below this card's 30 % floor) must print its refusal block and `ИТОГ`, not just exit 1. Audit the class: `grep -n "return [01];" <file>` inside a `try` whose function prints after the `finally`.
**Trigger:** about to add an early `return` inside a `try` in a tool that prints a block list / summary → check where that printing lives; if it is after the try/finally, move it inside the `finally` first.
**Not for:** functions that return data to a caller which does the printing — there the caller's path is the one to check, and an early return is perfectly fine.

### EXP-0028 · 2026-08-10 · ❌→✅ · #readback #instruments #mechanical #observation #gpu #generalization
**Context:** the project's first fan write (manual level 60 % through NvAPI's client fan-cooler interface), verified by the rule this project already owned — EXP-0014: *a written value counts as read back only when two consecutive samples agree.*
**Tried / did:** wrote the level, polled `nvidia-smi fan.speed` until two consecutive samples agreed, and asserted the settled value was at least the card's floor.
**Result:** ❌→✅ green, and **wrong**. Two runs of the IDENTICAL command disagreed by a factor of two: read ~2 s after the write the coolers reported **768 / 824 / 768 rpm** (`fan.speed` 30 %), read ~14 s after it they reported **1856 / 1861 / 1877 rpm** (61 %, matching the commanded 60 %). Nothing differed but WHEN the reading was taken — the 14 s run only happened because a parser bug was burning all 20 poll attempts on NaN. So the fan RAMPS, two consecutive samples had agreed on a plateau ON THE WAY UP, and my predicate ("at least the floor = 30 %") was satisfied by 30 % against a commanded 60. Fixed by making the read-back require the TARGET as well as stability (`config.FAN_LEVEL_TOLERANCE_PCT` = 8 pp, `FAN_RAMP_TIMEOUT_MS` = 30 s, both taken from this measurement), plus an rpm cross-check against the card's own `maxRpm` from `GetInfo`.
**Lesson:** **"read until two consecutive samples agree" proves a settled DIGITAL state and does not transfer to a MECHANICAL one — a ramping quantity has plateaus, and a plateau agrees with itself.** This is EXP-0020's shape one level up: there an instrument's OBSERVABILITY had a validity range, here a VERIFICATION RULE does, and both were generalized from the one case where they were first proved (a clock lock, which really does flip). So when reusing a read-back discipline on a new quantity, ask what kind of thing it is: a register flips, a fan spins up, a temperature drifts, a voltage settles — only the first is served by agreement alone; the rest need the target and a timeout long enough for the physics. Second half, about the predicate: `>= floor` passed for a neighbouring reason and taught nothing (EXP-0016), while `|actual − commanded| ≤ tolerance` can only pass for its own. **And the accident is worth keeping: the reading that exposed this came from a BUG** — a parser returning NaN made the poll run 14 s instead of 2, which is the only reason the ramp was ever seen. Read your tool's output as a stranger and the accidents show up as findings (EXP-0012).   → link: `researches/05` §9.4 · `config.FAN_LEVEL_TOLERANCE_PCT` · `plans/04` §4.7
**Repro:** `npm run nvapi -- --fan-write 60` — the block «скорость ДОШЛА до заказанных 60 %» must report a value within 8 pp of 60 and name how many probes it took (measured: 12 probes ≈ 8 s). A run that reports 30 % and passes means the target requirement has regressed.
**Trigger:** about to verify a write by reading a value back → name what KIND of quantity it is; if it can ramp, drift or settle, require the target and a physics-sized timeout, not just agreement.
**Not for:** discrete state a driver flips (a lock, a mode, a limit, a bit) — there agreement IS the proof and adding a target check buys nothing.

### EXP-0027 · 2026-08-10 · ❌ · #planning #process #drift #canon #phases
**Context:** a full day inside phase 4 (the NVAPI bridge) and, by the end of it, inside phase 5 (the ascent and the watt measurement). Plans existed on disk for phases 1 and 2 only.
**Tried / did:** worked from `STATUS.md`'s "next step" line and from `researches/`, writing the plan nowhere. Every individual step was grounded — a research anchor, a measurement, a guard — so nothing felt like improvisation from the inside.
**Result:** ❌ the owner asked *«планы под это у тебя написаны операционные? или ты импровизируешь? нужно работать по плану»*. He was right: `ls plans/` showed 01 (epic), 02 (phase 1), 03 (phase 2) — nothing for the phase I was executing or the one I had wandered into. Three costs, all of which a plan catches BEFORE the diff instead of after: the search method was wrong for half a day (per-point instead of the industry's raise-and-cap, found only when he asked whether this is what the internet describes); phase-5 work ran with phase 4 unclosed and no phase-5 plan; and phase 4's entry gate — "phase 3 exit gates green" — was waived by his verbal redirect and written down nowhere.
**Lesson:** **a chain of individually-justified steps is not a plan, and it feels exactly like one from the inside.** That is the whole trap: each step cites a research doc, each result is measured, each guard is proven — so the session experiences rigour and produces drift, because what is missing is not care but the ARTIFACT that fixes scope, order and exit criteria before the work starts. `AGENT_GUIDE.md` already names the mechanism ("quote the anchor line you are about to execute; if you can't name the line, that's scope drift") and it only works against a plan that EXISTS. Two operational tells worth watching for, both present here: **a phase entry gate waived by a chat message and never written into a document** (the waiver is legitimate — his word outranks the plan — but an unrecorded waiver erases the debt it created, and phase 3 is still undone), and **work that belongs to phase N+1 executed "while we're here"**. Write the phase's operational plan BEFORE its first line of code, and when a phase is entered out of order, record the waiver in the plan on the same day.   → link: `plans/04_epic01_phase4_nvapi_bridge.md` §0 and §5 · `plans/01_EPIC` §4 (phase gates)
**Repro:** `ls plans/` and match it against `MASTER_PLAN.md`'s open phases — every phase in flight must have `plans/NN_epicMM_*.md`. Before the first edit of a phase, grep that plan for the step you are about to do and quote its line; no line means either the plan is incomplete or the work is drift.
**Trigger:** about to write the first line of code for a phase, or noticing the work has crossed into the next phase → stop and check `plans/` for that phase's operational plan; if it is missing, write it first.
**Not for:** small tasks inside an already-planned phase (the plan's own steps are the unit there), and genuine one-liners — `/plan-task`'s heaviness test still applies, and ceremony must never outweigh the work.

### EXP-0026 · 2026-08-10 · ❌ · #scope #owner #questions #methodology #competence
**Context:** having proved the addressed GPU write, I closed the report by asking the owner which curve point the first undervolt should start from, offering him two routes.
**Tried / did:** framed it as a fork worth his input, because it felt consequential and I did not want to decide it alone.
**Result:** ❌ *«ты задаёшь мне вопросы, в которых я некомпетентен. Для этого KAGO и разрабатывается. Для этого я тебя просил провести ресерч и изучить формализованные методологии. Зачем ты меня этим грузишь?»* The answer already existed in the project's own evidence base — `researches/02` is the Vmin search methodology and phase 5 is its plan. I had a documented method and asked anyway.
**Lesson:** **"I don't want to decide alone" is not the test for escalating to the owner — COMPETENCE and AUTHORSHIP are.** The canon splits questions into vision-level forks (`/interview`) and everything else (decide and report), and the split is about WHO OWNS THE ANSWER: brand, UX, product shape and budgets are his; method, parameters, search order and thresholds are the agent's, and on a project built to spare him exactly this expertise, handing him a methodology question inverts the product's purpose. The tell is cheap: **if the answer could come from a research document, a standard, or a measurement, it is not a question for the owner** — it is unfinished work wearing a question mark. Deference feels safe and reads as offloading; a wrong methodological call is cheap to reverse and the owner can veto the RESULT, while an unanswerable question costs him time and tells him the agent has not read its own research.   → link: `researches/02` · `AGENT_GUIDE.md` → "Decisions the agent must NOT make alone" · MASTER_PLAN phase 5
**Repro:** before putting anything to the owner, run two tests on it. Test one — a document, a standard or a measurement could settle it: then go read that instead. Test two — the answer defines brand, UX, product shape or a budget: only then is it his. Fail test two and pass test one, and the item is unfinished work, not an escalation.
**Trigger:** about to ask the owner anything → run the two questions above; only a "no / yes" pair earns an interview.
**Not for:** genuine vision forks and taste criteria — those still go to him, and under-asking there is its own defect (`AGENT_GUIDE.md` → the taste class).

### EXP-0025 · 2026-08-10 · ❌→✅ · #testing #false-green #forensics #test-pollution #fixtures
**Context:** building the watchdog that returns the card to factory when the writing process dies. Its selftest fires the guard loop many times over injected fixtures in a temp file; the live drill then checks that a real firing leaves a report on disk.
**Tried / did:** ran the selftest, the mutation suite and the drill. All green — 19 blocks, 5 mutations, 7 drill blocks.
**Result:** ❌→✅ then I opened `runs/watchdog/` and found **23 "the card was reset in anger" reports, most of them written by the SELFTEST.** The loop wrote its report to the module-level directory regardless of which file the caller passed, so fixtures named "мёртвый владелец" and "просрочка" were manufacturing forensics. Worse, the drill's block *«сторож оставил отчёт»* only asked whether the directory was non-empty — it was, from that pollution, so **it would have passed with a completely mute guard.** Fixed by deriving the report path from the record's own path, sandboxing the selftest, adding a block that asserts the real directory did not grow, and making the drill require a FRESH report whose text matches this drill.
**Lesson:** **a test that writes into the product's real state does not merely risk flakiness — it FABRICATES EVIDENCE that a later session will read as history.** Run artefacts (crash reports, incident logs, alert records) are read by humans as testimony about what the machine did; a fixture that lands among them is indistinguishable from the real thing after the session ends, and nothing ever flags it. So: every artefact path a module writes to must be DERIVED from the input it was given, never taken from a module-level constant — that one habit makes test isolation structural instead of remembered. Second half, the one that keeps recurring here: **"the artefact exists" is not "my code produced it".** Capture the set BEFORE, require something NEW, and match it to this run — otherwise the check is satisfied by history (EXP-0016, third time this class has bitten in this project).   → link: `automation-engine/lib/watchdog.mjs` · `runs/watchdog/`
**Repro:** `ls runs/watchdog/fired-*.json` must print nothing after `npm run watchdog -- --selftest`; the suite's own block «отчёты подставных срабатываний НЕ попали в настоящую папку» asserts the same thing and fails if the sandboxing regresses.
**Trigger:** writing a test for anything that persists a record (a log, a report, an alert, an incident) → give it its own directory derived from a parameter, and assert the production directory did not grow.
**Not for:** pure in-memory tests, and artefacts written to an OS temp dir that nothing in the project ever reads as evidence.

### EXP-0024 · 2026-08-10 · ❌→✅ · #api #silent-failure #status-codes #verification #reverse-engineering #gpu
**Context:** KAGO's first ADDRESSED write to the V/F curve — one point, one mask bit — after the record's stride, offset field and unit had all been measured.
**Tried / did:** wrote the offset and checked the returned status. Status 0. Twice, across two different buffer-building strategies.
**Result:** ❌→✅ both writes were **accepted and completely inert** — not one byte of the 9 248 changed. The cause was neither the field nor the mask: the entry array starts at **0x44**, not at 0x20 right after the header, so the value was written 0x24 bytes before the address the driver reads. Found by asking a READ-ONLY question instead of guessing a third time — request the structure with a single mask bit for point 64 and see where the answer lands; it landed in slot 65, one entry late. With the base corrected the write works and the mask isolates, proved at three points (20, 64, 110): exactly one entry changes, the curve moves only there, the rollback returns every byte.
**Lesson:** **a malformed SELECTION is not a malformed CALL — so an API can return SUCCESS for a write that addressed nothing.** Version wrong, size wrong, mask illegal: all of those get you an error code, which is why they feel like the whole risk surface. An in-range index pointing at the wrong slot is a perfectly well-formed request to do nothing, and there is no code for that. **The consequence is the part that matters here: for any write, "status 0" is not the verification — the verification is READING BACK THE THING YOU CHANGED.** Code that trusts the status would have reported a profile as applied while the card ran stock, which on this project is the exact fraud class `/fable-judge` hunts. Second half, about diagnosis: when a write is accepted and inert, stop theorising about the VALUE and interrogate the ADDRESSING, and do it with a read — a Get call with a deliberately narrow selector tells you the array geometry for free and changes nothing. Third half, about credit: I changed two things at once (read-modify-write AND the base) and it worked, so I ran the variant that isolates them — the zero-filled buffer works identically, meaning RMW was never the fix. **A fix that coincided with the real fix will happily take the credit and become folklore.**   → link: `researches/05` §8.7 · `CLK_VF_CONTROL_DATA_OFFSET` · `--prove-mask`
**Repro:** `npm run nvml -- --probe-mask` — asks one structure with three masks (all bits / none / one) and reports which slot the single-bit answer lands in; the off-by-one is visible directly. Then `npm run nvapi -- --prove-mask 64 -15` must show exactly one entry changed and a byte-for-byte rollback.
**Trigger:** a write returns success and the state did not change → suspect the ADDRESS, not the value, and probe the geometry with a narrow read before writing again.
**Not for:** APIs that genuinely validate indices (most documented ones do), and cases where the state legitimately cannot move — here a negative offset on a point already at the clock floor writes correctly and changes no frequency, which is a THIRD outcome that must be told apart from both success and no-op.

### EXP-0023 · 2026-08-10 · ❌ · #canon #language #audience #chat #owner #decision-point
**Context:** a full `/resume` canon pass, then a session of phase-4 work, then the report to the owner in chat. The Languages rule was READ that session: its table puts «every chat report» in the owner's language.
**Tried / did:** wrote every chat message of the session in English — the language the working documents around me were written in.
**Result:** ❌ the owner had to ask *«а с каких пор ты решил говорить со мной на английском языке? Я просил?»*. Second time this project's agent broke the same rule and the owner caught it himself (EXP-0006 was the first, on the epic meta-plan). The rule was not forgotten and not misread — it was READ AT SESSION START and then never consulted again, because nothing asks the question at the moment a message is sent.
**Lesson:** **a rule that governs an act you perform HUNDREDS of times per session cannot live only in a document you read ONCE per session.** The canon pass loads the rule into context; the context then fills with the task, and by the time the act happens the rule is a memory rather than a check — the same decay `AGENT_GUIDE.md` → Context refresh describes for the canon, applied to a rule with a very high act-rate. The tell is specific and worth naming: **I was surrounded by English artifacts** (the guide, the frameworks, the code comments, the research doc — all correctly English, because the agent reads them) and drifted into the register of what I was reading instead of the register of who I was addressing. Language is routed by AUDIENCE, and the audience of a chat message never changes mid-session. Second half, about what to do rather than what happened: this one CANNOT be mechanized — the text being checked is my own reply, it never lands on disk, and no repository tool can see it (`AGENT_GUIDE.md` says exactly this about the neighbouring "showing is an action" rule). So the fix is placement, not tooling: the rule moves to the DECISION POINT (the first line of the Languages section and the `/resume` announcement step), phrased as the question to ask before sending, not as a table to have read.   → link: `AGENT_GUIDE.md` → Languages · EXP-0006 · bugs/KAIF/03
**Repro:** before sending any chat message, ask the one question the rule IS: *who reads this?* The owner → his working language (`.kaif/kaif.json` → `language`, here **ru**). Only the agent → English. Audit a past session in one command: `git log --format=%s` is agent-facing and stays English, while any report addressed to the owner must be ru.
**Trigger:** about to send a message to the owner in chat → route it by audience, not by the language of the files you have been reading.
**Not for:** the documents themselves — the routing table already governs those correctly, and agent-only artifacts (this file, the frameworks, code comments, commit messages) stay English on purpose.

### EXP-0022 · 2026-08-10 · ❌→✅ · #reverse-engineering #undocumented #instruments #observation #gpu #layout
**Context:** phase 4 was stalled on one question — which field of NvAPI's undocumented `ClkVfPointsSetControl` record carries the frequency offset. The source (LACT #936) said "72-byte entries, offset at the entry's start"; following it, the first dword turned out to be a flag, and the only non-zero word in all 9 248 bytes had been there before we wrote anything. Writing a real offset next would have been guessing at the owner's hardware.
**Tried / did:** stopped guessing and looked for a DOCUMENTED API that moves the same state. NVIDIA's NVML has one (`nvmlDeviceSetClockOffsets`), and `researches/05` §5.5 had already recorded — as a HAZARD — that NVML and NvAPI clobber each other. Applied a known offset through the documented call, re-read the undocumented struct before and after, and subtracted. Then made the tool DERIVE the geometry from the changed addresses instead of reading it off the screen.
**Result:** ❌→✅ the entry stride is **0x24, exactly half** the published 0x48, and the offset sits at **+0x14**, not +0x00 — which is why the old reader saw a "flag": at double the stride it was reading every other entry's wrong field. The arithmetic closed the old mystery too: 0x20 + 128 × 0x24 = **0x1220**, the exact address of that stray `1` — never a curve field, just the first word past the array. Confirmed at two magnitudes (−100 MHz → −100000 in every field, −37 → −37000), bounded by a memory-lever run that changed zero bytes (so the struct is graphics-only), and every run ended with a byte-for-byte return to the pre-write snapshot. A free bonus: the same NVML call publishes the **allowed offset range** (−1000…+1000 MHz) that `ClkDomainsGetInfo` had refused across 30 size/version pairs.
**Lesson:** **when a documented API and an undocumented one share hardware state, that shared state is a RULER — the hazard and the instrument are the same fact read in two directions.** A "these two clobber each other" warning is normally filed as a thing to avoid; it is also the only way to put a KNOWN value into an UNKNOWN structure and see where it lands, which converts reverse-engineering from guessing into subtraction. Two conditions make it honest: the lever must be documented (so its value is known, not inferred), and the instrument must be QUARANTINED in writing — ours can never become a backend, or the hazard comes back as a product defect. Second half, and it is the cheaper half: **derive the layout with code, not with eyes.** The published 0x48 is plausibly just 2 × 0x24 misread from a hex column by whoever wrote it up, and I would have made the same mistake scanning `0x58, 0x7C, 0xA0` by hand — `PHILOSOPHY.md` → «code before cognition» applies to reading a diff, not only to building features.   → link: `researches/05` §8 · `automation-engine/lib/nvml.mjs` · `CLK_VF_CONTROL_STRIDE`
**Repro:** `npm run nvml -- --find-offset-field -100` then `-37` (a non-round second magnitude tells a unit from a coincidence), and `--mem` to bound the domain. The guard is `npm run nvml -- --verify-decode`: it reads ONE buffer through both layouts and requires the published one to go red.
**Trigger:** about to write into an undocumented struct whose field positions come from someone else's card → first look for a documented API that moves the same state, apply a known value through it, and diff — before any write of your own.
**Not for:** structures nothing else can move (there the honest move is to say the layout is unverified and keep writes zero-valued), and cases where the documented lever changes MORE state than the field you are hunting — then the diff is ambiguous and you have learned nothing.

### EXP-0021 · 2026-08-10 · ❌→✅ · #owner #criteria #requirements #paraphrase #executable
**Context:** the owner's acceptance criterion for the `Max Optimal` profile — «найти перегиб кривой», the point where giving up more performance stops paying.
**Tried / did:** restated it in my own words each time it came up, and moved on to the work.
**Result:** ❌→✅ he had to say it **five times**, and my fourth restatement wrote *«целевая функция — ноль потерь»* into the project canon. That was wrong in a way that is easy to miss: on the axis I had measured, the knee HAPPENED to land at 0.1 %, so my paraphrase agreed with the data and disagreed with the criterion. On the next axis it would have sent the search to the wrong point. Fixed by making the criterion a FUNCTION — `findKnee()`: marginal return per percent given up, the knee is where that return collapses, the 5 % is a wall and not a destination. Seven blocks, three mutations, and the owner's own curve as a fixture.
**Lesson:** **when the owner repeats a criterion, the repetition IS the defect report — stop paraphrasing it and make it executable.** A paraphrase drifts a little each time and there is no diff to catch it, whereas a function is one artifact both sides can point at, and a fixture built from the owner's own numbers is a criterion he can check without reading code. The tell is cheap to watch for: **the second restatement is the signal**, not the fifth. Generalize past acceptance criteria — any rule the owner keeps re-stating (a naming convention, a review step, a tone) is a rule the prose is failing to carry, and KAIF already says this about repeated LESSONS («two strikes → a mechanism, never a third reminder»); this entry extends it to repeated OWNER CRITERIA.   → link: `AGENT_GUIDE.md` → Notes from the human · `findKnee()` in ladder-descent.mjs
**Repro:** `npm run descend -- --selftest` — the knee blocks run the owner's measured curve and assert 2692 MHz, plus a smooth curve with no knee and a generous one that must still stop at the 5 % wall.
